Asian Markets Slide as Anthropic’s Debut Fails to Lift AI Stocks

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

MSCI’s broadest Asia-Pacific index outside Japan fell 3.3% on November 20, 2026, leaving Asian stocks on track for their worst week since 2020 after Anthropic’s Nasdaq debut failed to bolster confidence in the wider AI trade, Reuters reported.

The move took the benchmark’s weekly loss to 8.5%, its worst weekly performance since March 2020, when COVID-19 was spreading globally, Reuters said.

Japan’s Nikkei lost 5%, bringing its weekly decline to 7.7%. South Korea’s KOSPI fell 5.2% and ended the week down 8%. Taiwan shares were down 6.2% on the day and 17.5% for the week, which Reuters said was their largest weekly decline since 1990.

The selling in Asia came after both the S&P 500 and Nasdaq Composite lost more than 2% on November 19, 2026, despite a surge in Anthropic’s shares during their debut. Reuters described Anthropic as the world’s first publicly traded frontier AI company. Nasdaq’s historical data show that the Class A shares opened at $111.50, traded between $92.72 and $115.10, and closed at $97.17.

“Risk aversion intensified as investors continued to unwind crowded positioning and reassess the broader macro backdrop following Anthropic’s record-shattering IPO,” said Citi markets analyst Preston Pagli. He added that Nvidia’s earnings had not provided the reset markets wanted, and that sentiment deteriorated further when Anthropic’s debut failed to spark a broader rally.

Reuters identified three existing pressures on AI stocks: Jensen Huang’s departure from Nvidia, momentum behind the anti-data-center movement following Democrats’ sweep of the US midterm elections, and concerns about rising debt loads across the sector.

Goldman Sachs analysts said no single headline drove the retreat. Asset managers and hedge funds were net sellers of macro products while buying selected consumer and financial stocks, and poor liquidity exacerbated the price movements. That account points to broad repositioning as a key mechanism behind the steep index declines.

Euro Stoxx 50 futures were down 1.7% at 6,017 at the time of the report. S&P 500 futures fell 0.5% and Nasdaq futures declined 1% during Asian trading, indicating that selling pressure was extending beyond the region’s cash markets.

Reuters reported that the dollar index was steady at 103.86 and heading for a fourth consecutive weekly gain after reaching its highest level since April 2025 during the same week. The 10-year US Treasury yield fell for a fifth straight session to 5%, while the two-year yield touched a 10-day low of 4.7216% as investors moved into government bonds.

The contrast between Anthropic’s strong debut and the broader retreat separates demand for an individual listing from confidence in the sector as a whole. For private AI companies and their backers, that distinction is consequential: public investors embraced a single offering while reducing exposure to the wider sector, with data-center opposition and debt concerns still unresolved.

Shares in Japan's SoftBank Group closed 14.48% lower on Friday, marking their worst day since August 2024, as a broader sell-off swept through Asian technology stocks following the highly anticipated market debut of artificial intelligence startup Anthropic.

The sharp decline underscores growing investor concerns over SoftBank's aggressive bets on artificial intelligence, particularly its exposure to OpenAI, as markets question whether massive investments in AI infrastructure will generate sufficient returns.

The group is one of the biggest backers of OpenAI, Anthropic's rival, having invested a total of $64.6 billion in the ChatGPT maker.

SoftBank's heavy exposure to OpenAI as well as its ownership of chip designer Arm has made its shares particularly sensitive to shifts in sentiment toward the AI sector. The stock lost 30% in October and is down 31% so far this month.

Japanese semiconductor stocks also tumbled, with Tokyo Electron closing 7.06% lower, Lasertec shedding 9.21% and Renesas Electronics losing 7.02%.

Elsewhere in the region, Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co, or TSMC, closed 4.76% lower in Taiwan.

The declines in Asia come after the tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite fell more than 2% overnight following Anthropic's highly anticipated Wall Street debut.

OAuth Consent Phishing: How Malicious Apps Pass MFA and How to Shut Them Down in Entra ID

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 20 • Originally published at iamdevbox.com

OAuth consent phishing never asks for a password. The attacker registers a multi-tenant application, sends the victim a link to the real login.microsoftonline.com authorize URL, and waits for the user to click Accept. Every step the victim sees is genuine, including MFA, which is why this attack class keeps working against organisations that think they are covered by phishing-resistant authentication. What matters is not who the user is, but what they just authorised an unknown app to do.

This guide covers how the attack works at the protocol level, the Entra ID consent settings that close it, a KQL detection you can deploy today, and the exact commands to clean up after a successful grant. For the related token-theft pattern that abuses RFC 8628 instead of the authorization code flow, see OAuth Device Code Flow Security.

The attacker's app is just an ordinary registration with a redirect URI they control. The lure is a standard authorization request:

From then on the attacker calls Microsoft Graph as the app. The grant is independent of the user's password and sessions, so a password reset does not remove it. This is the same refresh-token persistence problem seen in MFA bypass attacks, except here Microsoft itself issued the token to the attacker's client at the user's request.

Variants you will see in incident reports:

Microsoft documents three user consent settings in Entra ID: do not allow user consent, allow consent only for apps from verified publishers requesting low-impact permissions, or let Microsoft manage your consent settings (the default for new tenants, backed by the microsoft-user-default-recommended policy). Check which one your tenant is on before assuming you are protected.

Read the current assignment with Microsoft Graph PowerShell:

If this returns ManagePermissionGrantsForSelf.microsoft-user-default-legacy, users can consent to almost anything that does not require admin consent. Move to a stricter setting.

This is the strongest control and fits regulated tenants. Pair it with the admin consent workflow below, otherwise users hit a dead end and look for workarounds.

Note the weakness: it depends on the verified-publisher signal, and the permission classification (which scopes count as low impact) is something you must review rather than assume. Mail.Read should never be classified low-impact in a tenant that cares about mailbox confidentiality.

In the Entra admin center go to Enterprise applications > Consent and permissions > Admin consent settings and set Users can request admin consent to apps they are unable to consent to to Yes, then pick reviewers. Users get a request button on the consent prompt instead of a block, and reviewers see the app, publisher and exact scopes before approving. Make sure reviewers know to decline anything requesting offline_access plus mail or file scopes from an unverified publisher.

Microsoft's risk-based step-up consent turns a user consent request that Microsoft flags as risky into a requirement for admin consent. It only changes behaviour when user consent is enabled, and the BlockUserConsentForRiskyApps setting is controlled through the Graph PowerShell beta module with the Privileged Role Administrator role. Treat it as a useful extra layer, not a substitute for restricting consent: the flag is advisory and attackers iterate until their app is not flagged.

Entra ID writes a Consent to application audit event each time a user or admin grants consent. Forward the AuditLogs table to Microsoft Sentinel (or Log Analytics) and run:

Property positions in modifiedProperties vary by event shape, so run this once against a known consent in your tenant and adjust the index before turning it into an alert. Elastic's prebuilt rule uses the same event with a new-terms approach (first time a user and application pair is seen in the recent window), which keeps a campaign hitting many users visible because each pair alerts separately.

Microsoft's own guidance for investigating suspected grants is to search the audit log in Microsoft Purview for questionable "Consent to application" activities, and to review consent grants in large tenants on a weekly cadence. A scheduled review is cheap insurance.

Do not wait for an alert. List every delegated grant in the tenant and review it:

ConsentType of AllPrincipals means an admin granted it for the whole tenant, which deserves the most scrutiny. Principal means a single user consented. Resolve ClientId to the service principal with Get-MgServicePrincipal -ServicePrincipalId and check the publisher, creation date and sign-in activity. Apps nobody on your team can explain are candidates for removal.

A password reset alone leaves the attacker's refresh token valid. Run all four steps:

The equivalent REST call for step 1 is DELETE https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0/oauth2PermissionGrants/{id}. After cleanup, search the mailbox for inbox rules and forwarding the attacker may have created while they had Mail.ReadWrite, and review what the app read through the unified audit log so you can decide on disclosure.

Consent phishing succeeds because identity teams spend their effort on authentication strength and treat authorisation as a user decision. Passkeys and number matching do nothing here: the user authenticated correctly and then authorised the wrong party. The durable fix is making consent an administrative decision for anything beyond trivial scopes, and watching the audit log for the exceptions. If your org also exposes OAuth-based integrations to AI agents, the same principle applies to what those agents may be granted; see MCP OAuth 2.1 authentication and the OAuth 2.1 security best practices guide. To inspect the tokens an app receives during a test, use the JWT Decoder.

How AIClaw 3.12.13 Loads Selected Skills Before the First Model Request

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A skill catalog is useful when the agent needs to discover a workflow. The timing matters when the user already picked one. If the instructions arrive through a tool call, the first model request still has to decide to make that call from the catalog and the user's message alone.

AIClaw v3.12.13 gives an explicit selection a different path. This behavior ships in the tagged release, and the source links below point to that version.

Imagine a mounted skill named release-check and a request like “$release-check summarize these changes.” The relevant instructions can shape the model's first response or tool choice. There is no prerequisite load_skill round trip for the excerpt. If the user just asks for a summary, the model can discover an applicable skill from the catalog and call load_skill as needed.

The kernel matches complete $skill-name references against mounted skills. It appends the selected text to the outgoing request in memory. The stored user message keeps its original text. A retry after context compaction can still carry the selection, and the next turn starts with fresh selection state. See the request assembly and skill prompt code.

The catalog uses an estimated budget of up to 2,000 tokens, reduced to 2% of the configured context window when that yields a smaller budget. AIClaw shortens descriptions to retain more names. If names alone exceed the budget, it can omit entries. An exact reference to a mounted skill still works even if the catalog leaves it out.

Selected text has a separate limit of 8 KiB per skill excerpt and 32 KiB across selected blocks. An excerpt shortened by the byte limit directs the model to load_skill for more detail. These are prompt assembly limits. The catalog uses a token estimate, and supplying instructions gives no guarantee that a model will follow them.

The kernel regressions inspect requests captured by a test model. They cover first-request inclusion, turn scoping, a compaction retry, catalog limits, and UTF-8 truncation. Those checks establish what the kernel sends. They do not measure live-model instruction following, latency, or provider token usage.

What are the best SF Compute alternatives for H100 clusters?

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 20 • Originally published at nodus-compute.ai

The best SF Compute alternatives for an NVIDIA H100 cluster are Nodus Reserved Compute (Beta) for a quoted reservation from one GPU to a connected cluster, Together AI GPU Clusters for the lowest published short H100 reservation we found ($3.69 per GPU-hour), and Lambda 1-Click Clusters for 16 to 2,000+ GPUs on InfiniBand. Nodus also lists the H100 SXM at $2.60 per GPU-hour for on-demand jobs, which beats every cluster rate here when your work fits on one 8-GPU machine. SF Compute stays the better pick if you want to set your own price on an open orderbook and sell back hours you end up not using.

We build Nodus, so weigh this accordingly. Every competitor fact links to its own page, and every cost is written out so you can redo it with today's rates.

SF Compute sells compute time, not machines. Its docs describe pools that hold a schedule of node-hours, and orders that add or remove time from that schedule. One node is 8 GPUs, and you set a maximum rate in dollars per node-hour when you buy, per its orders docs. The alternatives below sell fixed terms at published or quoted rates.

Prices checked November 19, 2026: Nodus pricing, Nodus Reserved Compute docs, Together AI pricing, Lambda pricing, CoreWeave pricing, SF Compute specs and SF Compute orders docs. CoreWeave's figure is its North America on-demand price for the 8-GPU HGX H100 instance.

SF Compute does not publish a fixed H100 rate. Its pricing page asks you to sign in, and its orderbook API requires a Bearer token. The price you pay is whatever matches your order: buy orders fill if the book has supply at your max rate, and by default are cancelled if it does not, per the orders docs.

So the useful question is not "what does SF Compute cost" but "what rate does SF Compute have to beat". Multiply any per-GPU-hour price by 8 to get the node-hour rate SF Compute quotes in.

Before you place a bid on SF Compute, compare it with these four node-hour rates.

The decision rule we use: if SF Compute's best ask for your window is below the node-hour rate of the alternative you would otherwise sign, take it. If it is above, the published rate wins and you skip the bidding.

We priced one common cluster purchase: 64 NVIDIA H100 GPUs (8 nodes) for 14 days, which is 8 x 336 = 2,688 node-hours, or 21,504 GPU-hours. We picked two weeks because it is the shortest term Lambda's 1-Click Clusters accept. Storage, networking extras and taxes are left out everywhere.

Same sources and check date as above. The arithmetic: 21,504 x $3.69, 21,504 x $5.85 and 2,688 x $49.24.

The same two-week H100 cluster spans $79,350 to $132,357 at published rates, so the term you buy matters as much as the provider.

Nodus Reserved Compute does not publish a fixed price either. Its docs say each request is reviewed and answered with a proposal covering availability, price, access and delivery. Before you ask, the public rate index averages providers' published reservation rates for the exact term you pick, and shows nothing until at least two providers match. That gives you a market reference to hold any quote against, including one from SF Compute.

Often it does not, and that is where the biggest saving sits. A fine-tune or eval sweep that fits in 640 GB of H100 memory runs on one 8-GPU machine, so a cluster term pays for interconnect and hours you will not use.

Here is one 8 x H100 machine for 24 hours, 192 GPU-hours, at on-demand list prices:

Prices checked November 19, 2026, from the pages linked under the first table. The Nodus figure is the H100 SXM rate listed on the Nodus pricing page.

For work that fits on one machine, Nodus on-demand costs $499.20 a day, and SF Compute needs a fill below $20.80 per node-hour to match it.

On Nodus that run is a Job with a --max-cost spending limit, as the commands in the multi-node guide show. Nodus multi-node training is in Beta: gangs have an API ceiling of 8 nodes, and the actual size is limited by which provider pairs Nodus has qualified. For a 64-GPU InfiniBand run today, a reservation is the honest answer, and our reserved vs on-demand guide shows how to find the utilization where it pays off.

This is the one factor where SF Compute is different in kind, not just in price. Its reselling guide lets you place a sell order for any part of the time you own, including the end of a reservation or a gap in the middle. You receive credits only when a buyer fills it, minus a platform fee with a fixed per-node-hour part and a percentage part. Buy orders carry no fees, per the orders docs.

Take the two-week cluster above and suppose training converges on day 10. The last 4 days are 8 nodes x 96 hours = 768 node-hours, or 6,144 GPU-hours. At Together AI's $3.69 that is $22,671.36 of reserved time, and at Lambda's $5.85 it is $35,942.40.

Unless your contract says otherwise, a fixed term bills those days. On SF Compute you can offer them on the orderbook, and what you recover depends on the bid and the fee.

Two caveats from SF Compute's own docs. Reselling is in public preview, and its roadmap says preview features are best-effort and not covered by the uptime SLA. And sell orders are not guaranteed to fill.

SF Compute says plainly that nodes will go down. Its specs FAQ says it refunds failed nodes and can repack you onto nodes with healthy hardware. For your own state, its docs point you to object storage for saving training checkpoints between runs.

On Nodus the restart is part of the job. The multi-node guide says that if a member's machine is lost or reclaimed, Nodus stops the gang's current epoch, keeps the surviving machines, gets new ones only for the lost ranks, and restarts every rank with the latest checkpoint restored. recovery.maxAttempts counts those restarts and defaults to 3. The Nodus vs CoreWeave comparison walks through the same question for Slurm and Kubernetes users.

SF Compute is the better choice when you want price discovery instead of a rate card. You can bid below the current ask with --allow-standing and wait for a fill, which no published price list offers.

It is also the better fit when your plans change often. Selling back the end of a reservation, or a holiday week in the middle, is a documented feature with CLI commands. Callable buy orders, in public preview, let you reserve past your compute limit in exchange for letting others buy that time from you at up to 1.2 times your max rate, per the orders docs.

SF Compute buys you very short terms too. An order can be one node for one hour, and the specs page lists no ingress or egress fees, bare metal and managed Slurm, and InfiniBand on those clusters. Its multi-node guide runs torchrun across nodes over an isolated InfiniBand partition.

If you need 64 H100s on InfiniBand next Tuesday for 36 hours, SF Compute's model fits that request, subject to what the orderbook holds. Lambda's 1-Click Clusters start at two weeks, and our Nodus vs Lambda comparison covers when Lambda's longer terms make sense.

Keep your training script. SF Compute's multi-node guide launches torchrun on every node by hand over a subnet and InfiniBand partition. On Nodus, the gang is assembled for you and torchrun reads its rendezvous from environment variables Nodus sets, so it needs no flags.

Install the CLI and sign in, using the commands from the install docs:

Then run the two-node example from the multi-node guide. Start with the guide's smoke-test train.py, then swap in your own script:

With the guide's script, rank 0 prints world_size=2 when both nodes joined. For a reserved cluster instead, submit your GPU model, quantity, duration, location and start timing at Request reserved capacity. Submitting does not reserve inventory or authorize payment; Nodus confirms dates, configuration and terms first.

No. SF Compute is not one of the providers listed in the Nodus cloud accounts docs. You can use both side by side, but Nodus does not manage SF Compute capacity for you.

Buy orders have no fees; you pay the fill price when the order fills. Sell orders pay a platform fee with a fixed part per node-hour and a percentage of the sale value, per SF Compute's reselling guide.

Yes, accounts carry a compute limit in node-hours that counts both held time and unfilled buy orders. Its limits docs describe how standing sell orders and callable buy orders offset it, and how to request a higher cap.

Yes. The Reserved Compute docs say you can request a single GPU, and the final offer confirms how GPUs are arranged across physical servers. Console requests accept durations up to 365 days.

Humpback whale song's extra structure vanishes once its repeats are removed

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Earlier this week I reported that the "language-like" Zipf law found in humpback whale song (Arnon et al. 2025, Science 387:649) is matched by a simple second-order Markov chain. Three more pre-registered tests on the authors' own data have now come back, and together they trace the song's extra structure to its source: repetition.

First, a held-out prediction test (does knowing more than the last two elements help predict unseen song?) finds structure in whale song that a second-order chain cannot reproduce. The Zipf statistic could not show this.

Second, the song repeats itself far more than that chain does. Collapsing every tandem repeat (a cell of 1 to 8 elements sung twice or more in a row) to one copy removes 63% of the whale's elements, against 50% for the chains. 35 of the 80 most frequent whale "words" contain an immediate repeat or an x-y-x alternation, such as gr(s)-dws(s) sq sq sq.

Third, the decisive one: with the repeats collapsed, the held-out test finds nothing beyond a second-order chain. In all 8 years, looking 3 to 5 elements back predicts unseen song worse than looking 2 back. The test still had power: it found English words in 5 of 5 samples of the same size. And song made of second-order chains with random repeats added did not fool it (0 of 3), which we had not expected.

The structure behind the whale's "language-like" chunks is phrase repetition, which whale biologists have described since Payne and McVay (1971), plus short-range rules between elements. Because random repeats do not reproduce the signal, it seems to lie in how the whale repeats. One guess, not yet tested: a chain that remembers two elements cannot count to three, so a whale that sings a cell a set number of times would look like longer-range structure. None of this says whether humpback song has meaning.

The graders were published before the jobs ran and re-derive every label, prediction and score from the raw output. Read RESULTS-HOLDC.md on GitHub and the working manuscript.

One whale data set coded by human listeners, each year treated as one recording; one English corpus in phonemes. "With power" means power against structure as strong as English words; weaker long-range order could be missed. The surrogate null leans against detection (every negative control gave p = 1.000), so p values are conservative, not exact. 4 of 7 registered predictions came true in the last test (Brier 0.189).

Disclosure: this work was carried out with AI agents working in my name, and no human expert has checked it yet.

告别臃肿客户端：我们如何用现代 Web 技术打造零安装的“类微信”聊天工具 Welo小圈？

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

在日常开发与服务海外华人社群的过程中，我们发现了一个普遍却常被忽视的矛盾：通信技术越来越发达，但让人和人“建立连接”的门槛却越来越高。

每当你想组织一个兴趣小圈子、搭建跨境项目协作组，或者为本地生活服务提供一个在线咨询入口时，往往会卡在第一步：用户点开你的链接，却被弹窗要求“先去应用商店下载 150MB 以上的客户端、绑定手机号并完成繁琐验证”。结果就是，超过一半的潜在用户在下载进度条走完之前就已经流失。

与此同时，重度依赖中心化超级 App 还意味着随时面临算法干扰、账号风控限制，以及完全丧失数据自主权。

正是为了解决这些痛点，我们打造了 Welo小圈 (www.weloim.com) —— 一款主张“打开网页就能聊、体验像微信一样顺手、数据完全自主掌控”的轻量级网页版即时通讯（Web IM）应用。

The Landing, Beloved Los Angeles Gallery with a Keen Eye for Pairing Historical and Contemporary Artists, Closes After 14 Years

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Landing, a beloved Los Angeles gallery whose program was defined by its unique pairings of historical and contemporary artists, announced that it had closed. Its last exhibition was a one-night only solo for Maxwell Hendler staged on October 29.

On the gallery’s website, founder and owner Gerard O’Brien wrote, “the Landing Gallery at 5118 W Jefferson Blvd is now closed. We are no longer programming at this location. We are deeply grateful to the artists, collectors, collaborators, and community who have been fellow participants in our engagements. It has been a privilege to champion extraordinary artists, present ambitious exhibitions, and create a space for meaningful exchange and discovery.” Related Articles Two More Vienna Galleries Close, Citing 'No New Collectors' and 'Challenging' Financial Landscape Prairie, Chicago Gallery That Put Conceptualism Ahead of Commercialism, Closes After Nearly a Decade

In an Instagram post, Gerard O’Brien put it more simply, “End of an era … 22 years of Gallery spaces in Los Angeles, Reform Galley 2004-2019 … the Landing Gallery 2012 -2026 … onwards. thank you Los Angeles❣️”

O’Brien founded the Landing in 2012 as an off-shoot of his design-focused Reform Gallery. “I never really thought there was a big difference,” he said of the distinction between art and design. He chose the name for its original location, a spot within Reform: “he cleared out the landing atop a short staircase in his furniture gallery and dedicated it to shows of art, bringing in work by artists both contemporary and historical to display just above his design wares,” as a 2016 blog post by book publisher Phaidon reported.

In 2015, O’Brien decided that it was time for the Landing to grow, opening in a 2,800-square-foot gallery space on Jefferson Boulevard with a solo show for the late California artist JB Blunk.

Over the years, the Landing would become known for its pairings of historical and contemporary artists. At the time of its closing, its roster included four deceased artists (Jeremy Anderson, Richard Bowman, Leslie Kerr, and Fred Reichman) and five living contemporary artists (Yevgeniya Baras, Ryan Fenchel, Matt Phillips, Jonathan Ryan, and Amanda Valdez).

The gallery was also known for regularly staging off-kilter group exhibitions, with titles like “Rat Bastard Protective Association” (2016), “The Useful and the Decorative” (2017), “New Iconography: Artists Raising Children” (2021), and “Consensual Ennui (2023). The first of these, “3 Women” (2016) took its name from a 1977 Robert Altman film as a way “to create a cross-generational dialogue that explores the intersections of craft and fine art, influence and homage,” per a release. It featured the work of Lenore Tawney from the 1960s and ’70s paired with textile pieces by Tanya Aguiñiga and paintings by Loie Hollowell. The exhibition marked the first time Tawney’s work had been exhibited in LA in nearly 50 years.

In an Artforum review, Erin Kimmel wrote, “The Landing’s handsome, restrained exhibition showcased the exquisite detail of [Tawney’s] works, which reward prolonged scrutiny with seemingly boundless formal revelations.”

To mark its 10th anniversary at its Jefferson Boulevard space, the Landing mounted a two-part anniversary exhibition. The first part, which opened in November 2025, focused on contemporary art and design and featured the work of Ruth Asawa, Wallace Berman, Bruce Conner, Lyne Foulkes, and Doyle Lane. The second part, which opened this past April, featured gallery artists as well as others who had exhibited there, including Brenda Goodman, Sam Jablon, and Lukas Geronimas.

In a release for part one, O’Brien wrote, “10 Year brings together the feeling of the earliest days of the Landing Gallery when two years of exhibitions took place inside Reform Gallery on Melrose Avenue. Exhibiting art in a salon style in dialogue with decorative arts is how tL began.”

The Landing’s closure is the latest in an extended wave of closures that began in mid-2025. Los Angeles galleries have particularly been affected, with Murmurs, the Box, LA Louver, and Blum all shuttering, along with the LA outposts of Marian Goodman Gallery and Tanya Bonakdar.

In his statement on the gallery’s website, O’Brien added, “While our gallery doors have closed, our commitment to art and artists continues. Watch this space for news of future offsite exhibitions, activations, and special projects. Thank you for being part of the Landing’s ongoing narrative.”

Ice-T Dropped A Funny Take After Someone Didn’t Know He Was An Answer On Final Jeopardy

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ken Jennings Knows Exactly Who Should Replace Him On Jeopardy (And I Don’t Hate It)

Benson And Stabler’s SVU Reunion Stressed Me Out. Now I’m Wondering When We’ll See Christopher Meloni Again

Boy Meets World Star Says He’s Often NEXT To Danielle Fishel And People Don’t Recognize Him. I’ve Seen It Happen

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

It's an honor to be the answer to a question on Jeopardy!, and I'd assume it's an even higher honor to be the answer for Final Jeopardy! So, with that in mind, it's always fun to see how people react to being the subject of a question featured on the show. Case in point: Ice-T had a very funny take when he found out that someone didn't know he was the answer to Final Jeopardy!.

Final Jeopardy! can literally make or break someone’s future on the long-running game show. The clues can range from history to literature to, yes, even pop culture. On the episode from the 2026 TV schedule that aired on October 28, Ice-T was the answer for the final round. Two people got it right, but one person was wrong. Ice-T addressed the moment while appearing on the Today Show and revealed just what he thinks of the whole ordeal:

I’m in a lot of crossword puzzles, because Ice-T, I-C-E-T, is a weird spelled thing. But Jeopardy, I’ve been in Jeopardy before, but Final Jeopardy. …. [They asked] who put out an album, Home Invasion, and also is the longest-running character in television. And the guy said Tim Allen. I think he was thinking about Home Improvement, right? But the two women got it right, and I only care about the women [laughs]. So, you know, he lost, and they won. But that’s how it goes. But Final Jeopardy!’s a big deal.

Of course, being a clue on Jeopardy! is a big deal, even if it does sting when someone does not know the answer. And as Ice-T pointed out, he’s been in plenty of clues over the years. That being said, it’s a lot different when you’re in Final Jeopardy!

Overall, I love that Ice-T wasn’t too hurt over being mistaken for Tim Allen, and instead focused on the fact that the two women got it right, which is all he cares about. I'm also very entertained by the image of Tim Allen trying to rap.

Anyway, the clue was for the category “Current TV Streaks,” and it just shows that even if a clue seems easy, it may not be for a lot of people. For host Ken Jennings, there was one category that made him nervous when he was competing, and it was surprisingly hockey. While he wound up being one of the biggest Jeopardy! winners of all time and no one has yet to beat his 74-game streak, it sounds like that still haunts him. So, everyone has their blind spots.

Meanwhile, that Final Jeopardy! clue made me realize something truly insane. Despite the fact that Law & Order: SVU is in its 28th season, it’s wild to me that Ice-T has been playing Odafin “Fin” Tutuola for almost three decades. He’s been on the NBC drama since the second season and is one of the longest-running actors in the Law & Order franchise. Luckily, he doesn’t have any plans to leave any time soon. While he has been worried that he’d be written out as the show has had to make room for Kellie Giddish, that has not been the case.

Hopefully, the next time Ice-T is an answer for Final Jeopardy! everyone gets it right. However, even if it’s just the women, I’m sure he will be just fine. In the meantime, fans can catch him on Law & Order: SVU on Thursday, December 3, at 9 p.m. ET on NBC, streaming the next day with a Peacock subscription.

Il suo tifo per i rossoneri potrebbe rappresentare un ulteriore elemento a favore dell'operazione.

La Juventus ha ufficializzato il rinnovo di Augusto Sedorf Owusu fino al 30 giugno 2031. Il centrocampista classe 2005, cresciuto nel settore giovanile bianconero, ha collezionato finora 59 presenze con la Next Gen e ha già esordito in Prima Squadra il 17 settembre 2026, nella sfida di Europa League contro il NEC.

Diogo Dalot potrebbe tornare in Serie A. Il terzino portoghese del Manchester United, già protagonista con il Milan nella stagione 2020-2021, è seguito nuovamente dai rossoneri e dalla Juventus. Il giocatore, tuttavia, non sembra intenzionato a lasciare Manchester: nelle recenti dichiarazioni ha ribadito di voler vincere Premier League e Champions League con i Red Devils.

La dirigenza considera ancora fondamentale il centrocampista turco, anche perché Stankovic non sarebbe ancora pronto per raccoglierne l'eredità in cabina di regia. La decisione finale spetterà al giocatore.

Il giocatore, arrivato a Roma per circa 4 milioni, si è ritagliato un ruolo importante e ha un contratto fino al 2031. How Much Does It Cost To Earn United Premier 1K Status In 2026?

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ethan is an aviation professional with a degree in Aviation Management with Flight from the Florida Institute of Technology. He previously wrote aviation and tourism features for Florida Tech’s student newspaper, The Crimson, where he developed a clear, reader‑focused reporting style. Aviation journalism has remained a consistent priority throughout his career.

He currently holds a Restricted ATP and Commercial Multi‑Engine Pilot certificate and works in aviation supply chain and aircraft component repair. Alongside his industry role, he is pursuing his Certified Flight Instructor rating and actively building flight experience.

A lifelong aviation enthusiast, Ethan spent countless hours at Raleigh–Durham International Airport’s observation deck studying air traffic and practicing amateur photography and videography.

United Airlines' MileagePlus program launched on May 6, 1981, according to The Points Guy. The program, which celebrated its 45th anniversary this year, has undergone many iterations, particularly after the 2010 merger with Continental Airlines. According to NerdWallet, the program's most recent change took place on April 2, fine-tuning the mile-earning schedule for today's economy. Basic Economy officially no longer earns travelers miles, and it has reduced miles per dollar earned at each status level.

Premier 1K, United's top tier of earned status for passengers, grants a variety of benefits, amenities, and privileges onboard and in the airport. Premier 1K unlocks United's most comprehensive set of publicly available elite benefits, including priority pre-boarding, complimentary Premier Upgrades cleared as early as 96 hours before departure, three free checked bags, Economy Plus seating at booking for the member and up to eight companions, access to expanded award availability, a dedicated Premier phone line, and a bonus of 280 PlusPoints that can be used to request upgrades on eligible flights.

There are two main ways to achieve such status, including flying on United or United Express operated flights in a class other than Basic Economy, and with credit card spending with United's co-branded Chase credit card lineup. The exclusivity comes at a price, and not just anyone may qualify for Premier 1K, let alone United's entry level status, Premier Silver. For the 2026 qualification year, United Airlines illustrates on its website that earning Premier 1K status requires either 28,000 Premier Qualifying Points (PQP) or a combination of 22,000 PQP and 60 Premier Qualifying Flights (PQF).

PQPs are generally earned based on how much a traveler spends on United-operated flights, preferred seating, upgrades, and select partner airline tickets. The requirement makes Premier 1K one of the most expensive top-tier status levels among major US airlines.

A PQP measures the money you spend on airfare and eligible credit card purchases (typically earning 1 PQP per dollar spent on base fares). A PQF counts each individual flight segment you take on United, United Express, or eligible partner airlines. PQFs include layover segments. For instance, a round trip flight with a layover in each direction equals four PQFs earned.

The qualification requirements have steadily increased throughout the loyalty program's history. Following the merger with Continental Airlines and a broader airline industry trend toward revenue-based loyalty programs, United has increasingly prioritized spending over simply logging miles flown. Today, how much a passenger spends matters significantly more than how far they travel.

According to NerdWallet, one cannot simply earn Premier 1K status simply through spending on one of United's Chase cards. While a strong supplement in the pursuit of Premier 1K, it is not enough alone to suffice. United requires a four PQF minimum in order to qualify for any premier status, including the entry level Premier Silver.

While Premier 1K may traditionally be associated with road warriors flying hundreds of thousands of miles annually, United's current rules allow members to earn the vast majority of the required 28,000 PQP through credit card spending. The absolute maximum utilizing credit card spend requires 28,000 PQP, plus the four-flight minimum. Depending on the specific Chase card in use, one can earn 1 PQP per $20 spent or 1 PQP per $15 spent, translating to $560,000 or $420,000 in credit card spend annually.

In practical terms, a traveler pursuing status exclusively through credit card spending must supplement their spending with four PQF segments on United or an authorized partner airline. Business travelers whose employers pay for frequent premium cabin trips often reach the threshold naturally, whereas leisure travelers typically require a more deliberate strategy involving credit card spending and frequent travel.

This is certainly bad news for United MileagePlus members.

United's leadership has repeatedly emphasized that Premier 1K is intended to reward the airline's most loyal and highest-value customers. While MileagePlus has evolved significantly since its 1981 launch, the core philosophy remains centered around recognizing travelers who consistently choose United throughout the year. That philosophy is reflected in the program's modern qualification structure, where spending and engagement with the airline carry greater weight than mileage alone.

Jarad Fisher, President of United MileagePlus, joined United in February, having a proven track record as the Chief of Delta Air Lines' SkyMiles program, as well as being the creator of the Apple Card. The Points Guy has reported that Fisher describes Premier 1K earning in 2026 as majority PQP based, with United credit cards bridging the gap.

United continues moving toward revenue-based loyalty metrics. In October 2026, the carrier announced that Premier Qualifying Flights (PQF) would be removed from status qualification beginning in 2027, leaving Premier Qualifying Points (PQP) as the primary path to elite status. The change reinforces United's view that Premier status, particularly Premier 1K, should primarily reward customer value and spending rather than simply the number of flights taken.

Reaching Premier 1K is not solely about upgrades and priority boarding. United's broader loyalty ecosystem includes benefits such as PlusPoints, Million Miler status, and occasional status match opportunities from other airlines, all of which can influence whether pursuing 1K is worthwhile. For some travelers, these supplemental benefits may ultimately provide more value than the status title itself.

Possibly the most significant benefit is PlusPoints. Upon reaching Premier 1K, members receive a substantial allocation of upgrade credits that can be used on eligible United and partner-operated flights. Unlike complimentary upgrades, PlusPoints give travelers greater control over when and where they attempt to move into a premium cabin. However, upgrade inventory remains capacity controlled, meaning even top-tier members may not always secure their preferred seat.

Million Miler status adds another layer of value for long-term United customers. Unlike annual status qualifications, Million Miler recognition rewards cumulative lifetime flying with the airline. However, becoming a Million Miler is solely based on miles flown on a United Aircraft, not credit card spend. Overall, travelers considering a long-term loyalty strategy may therefore view Premier 1K as only one component of a broader relationship with United, especially compared with travelers who simply leverage status matches between competing carriers.

Why you now need a United credit card to get any real value out of MileagePlus.

Of course, airline loyalty is not solely dictated by its frequent flyer program, but a range of other factors such as geography, service quality, and price. However, for regular visitors to New York, Los Angeles, or even Chicago, when multiple airlines and airports are of choice, is there a better top tier airline status to compare?

At Delta, travelers earn Diamond Medallion status exclusively through Medallion Qualification Dollars (MQDs), with a threshold of $28,000 MQDs during the qualification year. The airline's program is almost entirely revenue-based, allowing members to earn progress through flights, Delta Vacations purchases, and eligible credit card activity. Meanwhile, American Airlines awards its highest published tier, Executive Platinum, after earning 200,000 Loyalty Points, which can be accumulated through flights, co-branded credit card spending, shopping portals, hotel partners, and numerous non-flying activities. As a result, many loyalty experts consider American's program the easiest top-tier status to earn because it offers the greatest number of pathways that do not require extensive flying.

A traveler based near a United hub may find Premier 1K considerably more useful than an equivalent status elsewhere due simply to flight frequency and network coverage. Meanwhile, a traveler primarily flying through Atlanta may derive greater value from Delta Diamond Medallion, while someone based in Dallas-Fort Worth International Airport (DFW) may prefer American Airlines Executive Platinum. In each case, the best loyalty program is often the one aligned with a passenger's most frequently used airport.

Discover the world's biggest airline loyalty programs, from United MileagePlus and Delta SkyMiles to Avios, LATAM Pass, and Flying Blue.

United Premier 1K remains one of the most prestigious airline status levels available in North America, but it is also one of the most difficult to achieve. Travelers must accumulate significant Premier Qualifying Points through flying, spending, or a combination of both while maintaining an active relationship with the airline throughout the qualification year. Premier 1K status brings travelers select privileges, such as checked baggage, United Club access, upgrades, and more.

MileagePlus's evolution illustrates a broader industry trend toward rewarding revenue contribution rather than simply distance flown. Benefits such as PlusPoints, priority services, expanded award availability, and upgrade opportunities can provide substantial value for those who travel regularly. Premier 1K status provides extra value for travelers based in or frequently visiting any of United's hubs, with the presence of native United Airlines staff, a United Club, and with mainline United service with upgrade ability.

Looking ahead, the trend toward revenue-based qualification appears unlikely to reverse. As airlines such as Delta, United, and American alike continue refining loyalty programs, travelers should expect qualification requirements and earning structures to evolve. As qualification thresholds continue to rise, Premier 1K increasingly represents not just frequent travel, but a substantial financial commitment to the airline and the broader MileagePlus ecosystem. A steady increase in the minimum threshold for Premier 1K has been defined by Thrifty Traveler as Pointsflation, keeping the bar high for frequent flyers. Only the most loyal United passengers have a shot at obtaining Premier 1K in today's earning environment.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Teamshares (TMS) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Acquisitions drove a sharp EBITDA jump as refinancing reshapes the balance sheet.

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Updated Nov 19, 2026 at 6:48PM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Teamshares (TMS +5.33%) reported year-over-year revenue growth and maintained its full-year pro forma adjusted EBITDA outlook. Management indicated that the company reached a financial inflection point following the transition to public markets, utilizing new capital to accelerate its programmatic acquisition strategy. The company is currently focused on refinancing legacy debt and scaling its tech-enabled platform to oversee a diversifying portfolio of small and medium-sized businesses. Management stated that the business model is designed to provide a permanent home for companies with retiring owners while leveraging internal software to maintain financial control and drive operating leverage across its subsidiaries.

Operator: Thank you for standing by, and welcome to Teamshares Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Call. At this time, all participants are in a listen-only mode. After the speaker's presentation, there will be a question-and-answer session. To ask a question, you will need to press star one on your telephone. You will then hear a message advising your hand is raised. To withdraw your question, please press star one again. Please be advised that today's conference is being recorded. Now it's my pleasure to turn the call to Mike Levin, Teamshares Investor Relations. Please proceed.

Mike Levin: Thank you, Carmen, and good evening, everyone. Welcome to Teamshares Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Conference Call. Joining me today are Co-Founder and CEO Michael Brown, Co-Founder and President Alex Eu, and CFO Brian Gaebe. Earlier today, we issued a press release announcing our financial results, which is available on our investor relations website, along with the supplemental slide presentation. As referenced on slide two, we will be discussing non-GAAP financial measures during this call. The most directly comparable GAAP financial measures and reconciliation of the differences between the GAAP and non-GAAP financial measures are available in our earnings release and supplemental slide presentation on our website.

In addition, certain statements being made today are forward-looking statements that are based on management's current assumptions, beliefs, and expectations concerning future events impacting the company. These forward-looking statements involve a number of risks and uncertainties, including but not limited to those described in our earnings release, Form 10-Q for the period ending September 30, 2026, and other filings with the SEC. The actual results of operations and financial condition of the company could differ materially from those expressed or implied in our forward-looking statements. With that, I will turn the call over to Michael. Michael?

Michael Brown: Thanks, Mike. In a recent press release, we announced that Teamshares established an investor relations function, and we're thrilled to have Mike Levin join our team. Mike brings deep IR experience from Carvana, having joined the company just a few months after it went public, where he built the IR function from scratch. Mike reports to Nile Corso, our Head of Capital Markets, and can be reached at [email protected]. Welcome to Teamshares Third Quarter Earnings Call for 2026. We're excited about what we've accomplished in the first 150 days since going public and look forward to completing a strong fourth quarter. Most importantly, we're excited about the multi-decade growth opportunity ahead of us.

Turning to slides three and four, as a quick recap for new investors, Teamshares is the market leader in buying great businesses from retiring owners. 4.5 million small to mid-sized businesses in America, and tens of millions globally, have owners approaching retirement. We plan to be a permanent home for thousands of the highest quality companies as their owners retire. Our capital allocation strategy is to continually reinvest operating cash flows into new acquisitions and high-return organic growth. Our diversified industry strategy leads us to a bigger addressable market, which we believe provides economic diversification and positive selection by having more opportunities than under a single industry strategy.

Yet, we still benefit from industry learnings and efficiencies as we gain density in a given market. We are at a key inflection point in the company's financial history, as Teamshares scales toward the $100 million pro forma adjusted EBITDA projection we have previously published for year-end 2027. Turning to slide five. At heart, we're an acquisitive operating company that builds software to create scale and financial control, but capital access is a key driver of our growth. On the last call, we discussed how the public company capital advantage is unevenly distributed among companies that use financing to drive core business growth.

Financing new acquisitions is the raw material of our growth, so being public is a strategic advantage for Teamshares. Since our last call, we've made several moves on the capital front. On September 23rd, we announced the preferred stock financing. This non-convertible capital blends together with lower-cost seller debt and senior debt financing to create an attractive blended cost relative to our returns on acquisitions. We also view the preferred stock as much cheaper than our own internal view of common equity, which carries a significant opportunity cost as we scale. On October 19th, we announced the refinancing of the legacy credit facility that was coming due in December this year.

We had a few avenues available to us, and among the paths with the highest execution certainty, we chose our path based on a mix of cost, terms, and perhaps most importantly, the flexibility to repay early. I say this last point because we anticipate our credit metrics will look quite different by the new year and through the course of 2027 than they did this past summer when last in the institutional debt markets. In the meantime, we chipped away with selective bank term loans, having funded one since quarter end with a mid-single-digit interest rate.

Brian will walk through later how our corporate senior debt profile is on the cusp of being at a 3x-4x gross EBITDA ratio basis, a transformative improvement from the double-digit level at year-end 2025. He'll also walk through the progress we've made on blended interest rate, improving to 10.5% on a pre-tax basis at quarter end, pro forma for the i80 refinancing and the previously disclosed paydown of legacy mezzanine debt. Now our attention is turning to additional senior debt, and we plan to engage an investment bank with a leading leverage finance practice to run an institutional process.

We continue to see a multi-year opportunity to lower our spread above benchmark rates through ever-increasing scale and time in the public markets. On the last call, we told you that most of our acquisitions this year will close in Q4, and that we'll communicate tracking to our $40 million acquired EBITDA through our backlog and closed acquisition stats. We're pleased to announce that as of today, we've acquired $20 million of estimated annual adjusted EBITDA in 2026 and have an LOI backlog of over $20 million of EBITDA, and additionally, verbal agreements on acquisitions of several more and potentially EBITDA. We felt that it was important to complete the $150 million debt refinancing before accelerating acquisition closings.

It was retired on October 16th, and Q4 closings have begun in earnest, with $13 million of adjusted EBITDA closed since the end of the last quarter. Given our pace of growth, we think it's important for investors to understand what we've closed between the period end and the earnings date, hence our subsequent events disclosure on acquisitions. We have the capital on hand to close acquisitions and are focused on working through diligence and legal processes to continually close the companies under LOI. We also continue to add additional LOIs and build our 2027 backlog.

We're in the market every day and working through an inbound funnel of over 15,000 acquisitions that come to us every year that meet our size criteria. Now I'd like to hand it off to Brian to talk through the financial results.

Brian Gaebe: Thanks, Michael. Turning to slide six, I wanted to highlight how we continue to deliver strong year-over-year growth in our key financial metrics. Alex Eu: Thank you, Brian. As we discussed last quarter, we believe that our business model and the way it compounds can be understood through the four financial levers that are summarized on slide eight. One, can you acquire accretive, durable businesses at attractive terms? Two, can you maintain and grow their earnings? Three, can you have those segment earnings substantially drop through the consolidated earnings through the operating leverage at our platform? Then finally, can you earn improved access to capital, including lowering its cost over time, which then feeds back to enable more acquisitions? This quarter, we made progress against those drivers.

In addition to that, we have signed non-binding letters of intent for businesses that are expected to contribute more than $20 million of annual adjusted EBITDA, and our focus for the rest of the year is to close these acquisitions and continue adding to the backlog for 2027. The next slide and driver is how we can maintain and grow earnings after acquisition. Our model does not require aggressive growth or synergies to make an acquisition attractive, but it certainly benefits from it and is an additional source of value creation over time.

In the third quarter, organic revenue grew 3.6%, while organic segment EBITDA grew 2.3% compared with the 0.4% growth in the second quarter that we reported. Timing differences and uneven quarters from individual businesses can create variance in quarterly growth rates, particularly when there's a smaller prior year base that magnifies percentage changes, which is why we believe that year-to-date performance provides a more meaningful view of underlying organic growth.

Now, a couple highlights here we want to talk about on the right is that because we buy from retiring owners, leadership succession is central to our model, and we have invested to build dedicated recruiting, training, and operating support to manage the transition and preserve the customer relationships and company value. These transitions may seem hard in the short term, but we believe it sets a better foundation for our permanent ownership model because we deliberately manage that transition upfront. Second, our diversified subsidiaries give us resilience and multiple paths to aggregate organic growth.

This dynamic has helped us deliver positive organic growth throughout 2025 and each quarter thus far in 2026, despite some uneven operating conditions in certain end markets. Third, the lessons we learn, or tuition as we like to call it, feed directly back into our acquisition selection and underwriting. The lessons also help us identify operating improvements that we can apply across other businesses we own. Fourth, our growing network allows us to systematically compare organic reinvestment opportunities across businesses and direct incremental capital to those we believe offer the most attractive risk-adjusted returns. Now, we flip to slide 11 on operating leverage.

A core thesis that we have had is that we've built a platform that allows us to acquire, consolidate, and oversee a large number of businesses at scale. Now, year-to-date segment EBITDA increased approximately $17 million, while consolidated adjusted EBITDA increased approximately $12 million, which means that for every incremental dollar of SME segment EBITDA generated during the first nine months of this year, approximately $0.72 flowed through to consolidated adjusted EBITDA. On a percentage basis, that's a 57% increase in segment EBITDA relative to a 9% increase in corporate costs. A few things to note on the comparison.

As we write in the footnote, that included a $2.7 million headwind in the year-to-date that reflects the divestiture of real estate in the previous year. Corporate costs also reflect a full quarter as a public company versus the prior year when we were private. The expenses this year also include costs from the financing alternatives that Michael mentioned that we ultimately did not choose to pursue. Finally, as Brian mentioned, and very importantly, these reported results do not include contributions from the businesses we acquired subsequent to the quarter end, which was another approximately $13 million of expected annual EBITDA.

Now, as more of our subsidiary earnings flow through to the consolidated profitability, both organically and through acquisition, we believe that our financial profile will improve and support greater access to capital on increasingly attractive terms. Now, with that, I'll turn it back to Brian to discuss the progress that we're making in those areas.

Brian Gaebe: Thanks, Alex. Slide 12 provides a high-level overview of our capital strategy. Our priorities are straightforward: establish scalable financing capacity to support acquisitions, improve our blended cost of capital, and bolster cash flow generation for reinvestment. The financing transactions completed in recent months are important milestones, but we view them as the foundation for a broader transformation of our capital structure. Over the next few slides, I will walk through how our recent financing transactions help further our strategic objectives and explain why we believe our growing earnings base and improving financial profile position us to access increasingly attractive financing options. Slide 13 highlights the evolution of our cost of debt and other efforts to optimize our debt stack.

We have made substantial progress reducing our cost of debt, and we believe there is a significant opportunity for further improvement. As you can see on the slide, our weighted average contractual interest rate has declined from 14.6% at the end of 2024 to approximately 10.5% on a pro forma basis at the most recent quarter end. That is a reduction of more than 400 basis points in less than two years.

This improvement is both the result of strengthening our financial profile as we continue to scale and a deliberate series of capital structure decisions, which include refinancing our legacy credit facility, retiring higher-cost mezzanine debt and other instruments with elevated cost, increasingly utilizing seller notes, and selectively introducing lower-cost senior debt financing at the operating subsidiary level. In the near term, our focus is obtaining debt financing against our growing base of unencumbered earnings that spreads below our primary existing term loan.

To put that near-term opportunity into perspective, predominantly all of the businesses acquired since the second quarter of 2026 remain unencumbered by senior debt, and we expect that to be the case in the near term for additional acquisitions currently under LOI as they close. This should allow us to introduce modest leverage at these businesses and recycle the proceeds into additional acquisitions while maintaining a disciplined approach to our overall capital structure. Slide 14 highlights another important development in our financial profile, which is the rapid improvement in our consolidated senior leverage.

Historically, our senior debt has been attached to the OpCo and holding company levels, where leverage at issuances typically ranged from 2x-3.5x EBITDA from the related business or set of businesses. However, at the consolidated level, our leverage ratios have historically been considerably higher after factoring in the investments in corporate infrastructure to scale our platform. These costs reduce consolidated EBITDA but are not included in the earnings calculations that support debt financing at the OpCo and holding company levels. The benefits of those investments are really starting to flow through our financial results. As Alex demonstrated earlier, segment EBITDA is increasing at a materially faster rate than corporate cost.

This operating leverage, together with our recent debt repayments, is driving a substantial improvement in our consolidated credit profile. As illustrated on the bottom half of the slide, we are targeting a reduction in our gross senior leverage ratio from 14x at the end of 2025 to approximately 3x-4x by year-end 2026. This reduction is expected to be driven by consistent to moderate changes in senior debt, coupled with the increased EBITDA due to acquisitions. This trend is particularly meaningful from a financing perspective.

As our consolidated leverage metrics become more closely aligned with the underlying leverage of our operating businesses, we believe our overall credit profile will become increasingly attractive to lenders and positions us to pursue greater senior financing capacity to fuel growth through acquisitions as we move into 2027. As we highlight in our earnings release, we intend to capitalize on our recent momentum by actively pursuing a debt financing process that supports our programmatic growth strategy. Moving to slide 15, I want to spend a moment explaining why we believe the preferred stock issuance is so fundamental to our growth strategy and creates significant opportunities for value creation.

We believe this was an attractive alternative to issuing common equity, providing capital to support growth while preserving future upside for our common shareholders. The proceeds provide us with substantial capital to fund growth through acquisitions, alleviating any capital constraints on any acquisitions closed since the issuance, our current backlog under LOI, and beyond. By combining preferred capital with seller notes and, when available, lower-cost senior debt, we believe our typical acquisition profile can generate attractive returns after their financing costs. Additionally, the instrument gives us meaningful flexibility in how we manage cash flow in our capital structure.

We can pay dividends in cash or in kind, which lets us direct near-term cash flow to where we believe it will generate the highest return for our shareholders. That can mean deploying capital into accretive acquisitions or retaining cash to strengthen our position in debt financing, including expanding our borrowing capacity. Those benefits can extend well beyond the immediate cash savings. We recognize that paying in kind increases the outstanding preferred obligation, and we will weigh that trade-off through the same disciplined capital allocation framework we apply to other opportunities. In summary, we view the pref as more than a source of liquidity. It supports acquisition execution today and creates flexibility to optimize the financing mix as we scale.

Turning to our 2026 guidance on slide 16, we believe the combination of our recent financing accomplishments and substantial acquisition backlog positions us to deliver significant earnings growth by year-end. As of September 30th, our LTM pro forma adjusted EBITDA was $23.1 million. We're targeting approximately $60 million by year-end, representing a substantial increase in our earnings over a relatively short period. We aren't relying on an aggressive organic growth assumption to achieve that guidance. The anticipated step change in earnings is predominantly driven by identifiable acquisition opportunities, with the operating model and corporate infrastructure providing the foundation to sustain and scale those earnings. Those are extremely flexible instruments, little to no covenants, and most of them carry a five-year term.

Anthropic’s record-setting IPO is expected to make many of its employees rich, and other AI companies may be next. At the center of this modern gold rush is San Francisco, a city used to booms and busts – but never at this scale. (Source: Bloomberg)

Tresoldi, Bruno Fernandes e altri: il piano di Cardinale per un Milan vincente prende forma

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Una strategia su due fronti. Da una parte il mercato di gennaio, dove Gerry Cardinale vuole consegnare a Ruben Amorim nuovi elementi funzionali al progetto tecnico. Dall’altra l’estate, quando potrebbe arrivare un colpo di grande prestigio come Bruno Fernandes, diventato uno dei nomi più intriganti per il futuro del Milan. Il portoghese, infatti, non rappresenta un’operazione immediata, ma una possibile occasione da cogliere nella prossima sessione estiva, quando potrebbe liberarsi. Il suo futuro al Manchester United resta in bilico dopo le difficoltà nelle trattative per il rinnovo e il Milan monitora la situazione.

Il legame tra Bruno Fernandes e Amorim potrebbe rappresentare un fattore importante. I due si conoscono bene e il centrocampista sarebbe il profilo ideale per aggiungere qualità, leadership ed esperienza alla trequarti rossonera. L'operazione resta però complessa: oltre alla concorrenza, pesa il valore economico dell’affare e la volontà dello United di provare a trattenere il proprio capitano. Diverso il discorso per Diogo Dalot, già passato dal Milan nella stagione 2020/21: il terzino portoghese piace per duttilità e conoscenza del campionato italiano, ma al momento non sembra intenzionato a lasciare Manchester, dove vuole inseguire obiettivi importanti.

A gennaio il reparto offensivo resta una delle priorità. Il Milan cerca una punta capace di raccogliere l’eredità di Francesco Camarda, destinato a un’esperienza in prestito per trovare maggiore continuità. I nomi sul tavolo sono quelli di Endrick e Nicolò Tresoldi. Il brasiliano rappresenta il grande talento internazionale, ma il suo cartellino richiede un investimento vicino ai 70 milioni di euro. Più giovane e in forte crescita è invece il profilo di Tresoldi, la cui valutazione è aumentata dopo un ottimo avvio di stagione (il Bruges è bottega cara). La scelta non sarebbe legata soltanto al costo, ma soprattutto alle esigenze tattiche di Amorim: il Milan vuole un attaccante capace di inserirsi in un sistema offensivo moderno e diventare un riferimento per il futuro. Insomma, tanti obiettivi di spessore per una squadra sempre più competitiva, come ha promesso la proprietà.

House China panel calls for cutting defense contracts over aerospace ties to Beijing's military

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Congressional watchdog leaders Randall Schriver and Mike Kuiken warn China could exploit American chips and AI to strengthen its military.

A new congressional report is urging the U.S. to restrict investments in China’s aerospace sector and withhold taxpayer funding from companies that build military capabilities for U.S. adversaries, warning that Western commercial partnerships transferred technology and expertise to Chinese firms with military ties.

The 65-page report, titled "China's Wingmen: How Western Government Policy Enabled China's Rise in the Skies," follows a year-long investigation by the House Select Committee on the Chinese Communist Party, led by Chairman John Moolenaar, R-Mich., and Ranking Member Ro Khanna, D-Calif. It examined how legal commercial partnerships transferred advanced technology and manufacturing expertise to Chinese military-linked companies.

China's People's Liberation Army (PLA) is the country's military, which the committee says has benefited from civilian aerospace technology transfers.

"American and European companies transferred technology to China that could be used against our brave men and women in uniform. With the CCP’s military-civil fusion there are no private companies in China," Moolenaar said in a statement shared with Fox News Digital.

CHINA COULD EXPLOIT US TECHNOLOGY TO THREATEN AMERICAN FORCES, CONGRESSIONAL WATCHDOG LEADERS WARN

China's domestically produced C919 passenger jet sits on display at the Singapore Airshow on Feb. 4, 2026, in Singapore. (VCG/VCG via Getty Images)

"The PLA will exploit civilian technology and use it to advance its military ambitions and that is what happened here."

The report singled out European aerospace giant Airbus, which receives U.S. defense contracts while maintaining manufacturing partnerships in China. Although Airbus sold its stake in an AVIC affiliate in 2025, its commercial partnerships with the Chinese aerospace conglomerate continue, according to the committee.

The bipartisan panel examined at least six U.S.-China joint ventures involving American defense suppliers GE Aerospace, Honeywell Aerospace and RTX subsidiary Collins Aerospace.

Investigators found that GE transferred foundational avionics technology to a joint venture with a subsidiary of AVIC, the Chinese state-owned conglomerate that develops military aircraft, including the J-20 stealth fighter. The committee said GE withheld its original joint venture agreement despite repeated requests.

RUSSIA IS HELPING CHINA CLOSE GAP WITH US FORCES AMID TAIWAN TENSIONS, WARNS LONGTIME MILITARY LEADER

An Airbus aircraft assembly facility in Tianjin, China, on Oct. 25, 2025. The European aerospace manufacturer opened its second A320-family final assembly line in the city that month. (VCG/VCG via Getty Images)

The American companies maintained that their Chinese partnerships supported civilian aircraft and complied with U.S. export controls. The report also cited previous export-control settlements involving all three companies but did not establish that the partnerships under investigation violated U.S. law.

"We appreciate the Committee’s work ensuring the U.S. retains its position as the world’s commercial aviation leader while protecting our most sensitive technologies. We have and will continue to follow relevant U.S. laws and regulations, as the report acknowledges,"a Collins Aerospace spokesperson told Fox News Digital.

The committee urged the Federal Aviation Administration not to certify Chinese-manufactured aircraft, citing state subsidies and Beijing's refusal to release a final report on the 2022 China Eastern Airlines crash.

"The report’s only recommendation directed at the FAA is that they do not certify Chinese-made aircraft, which the agency has not done," an FAA spokesperson told Fox News Digital.

A J-20 stealth fighter jet of the Chinese People's Liberation Army (PLA) Air Force performs at the China International Aviation and Aerospace Exhibition, or Airshow China, in Zhuhai, Guangdong province, China Nov. 9, 2022. (China Daily via Reuters)

A representative for Airbus did not immediately respond to Fox News Digital's request for comment.

Brittany Miller is a Fox News Digital breaking news writer and former Independent reporter who graduated from Syracuse University in 2023.

Ledger CTO Charles Guillemet to leave after nine years at company

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Guillemet helped establish Ledger’s Donjon security lab and says his departure has been planned for months.

Ledger chief technology officer Charles Guillemet announced he will be leaving the company at the end of 2026, after nine years at the company.

In a post on LinkedIn on Thursday, Guillemet said he joined as chief security officer in December 2017, around three years after Ledger was founded. He launched Ledger Donjon, the company’s attack lab, a year later.

“We’ve been preparing this transition for many months, so Ledger’s teams, partners and roadmaps are ready. I’m bullish on Ledger’s future,” said Guillemet.

Guillemet has been a central figure in Ledger’s security operations, first as chief security officer and later as chief technology officer. He helped establish the Ledger Donjon attack lab, which tests hardware and software vulnerabilities. According to Guillemet, Ledger devices now secure roughly 20% of the world’s crypto assets.

“The threat landscape changed too. AI is making attacks cheaper, faster and easier to scale,” he added.

“The question we spent a decade answering for crypto (how do you protect what matters when any device can be compromised?) is now a question for every company, every AI agent and every government.”

Guillemet did not disclose details of his next venture but said he was “building something new” and would “dedicate my time to solving this security mess.”

Guillemet’s announcement comes weeks after Ledger disclosed that attackers had modified genuine hardware wallets with hidden components designed to capture users’ recovery phrases, and slipped the devices into authorized resellers.

The tampering cases were initially reported in Asia, with subsequent incidents identified in Europe and the United Arab Emirates.

Voyager 1 team, unbowed by communications glitch, celebrates epic 1-light-day distance milestone

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Latest space missions, discoveries and exploration updates.

Stargazing tips, cosmic events and expert insights on the universe
