Discover this week's must-see night sky events, moon phases, and stunning astrophotos. Sign up for our skywatching newsletter and explore the universe with us!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

"I'm confident that it won't be too long, and it will send us data back."

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

PASADENA, Calif. – NASA's Voyager mission team cheered and wiped away tears here on Thursday (Nov. 19), celebrating a big milestone — Voyager 1 just reached one light-day from Earth, something no other spacecraft has ever done. The excitement persisted despite a communication anomaly that cropped up on the probe, which the team remains confident is temporary.

Voyager team members gathered for a press conference about the mission at the Von Karman auditorium here at NASA's Jet Propulsion Laboratory (JPL) — the first such Voyager event since 1989, when Voyager 1's twin, Voyager 2, flew past Neptune. The gathering served to celebrate Voyager 1's distance milestone: At 5:16 a.m. EST (1016 GMT) on Wednesday (Nov. 18), the probe reached one light-day from Earth — the distance a photon travels in one day, which is about 16 billion miles (26 billion kilometers). That means it now takes a full day for a signal to travel from Earth to Voyager 1, the farthest human-made object out in space.

"I remember in 1977 I was standing at the Cape watching Voyager launch, and I never thought I would be here today, 49 years later, to celebrate Voyager's one light-day. What an incredible milestone," Voyager project scientist Linda Spilker said during Thursday's event.

While this day and this week have been chock-full of excitement over the distance milestone, the Voyager mission team has been hard at work, as usual, keeping the two nearly 50-year-old spacecraft alive and functioning.

Unfortunately, as JPL spokesperson Raquel Villanueva shared at this celebration, on Saturday (Nov. 14), Voyager 1 stopped transmitting science and engineering data. The team has remained in contact with Voyager 1 while investigating the issue, and they are confident that they'll be able to solve it soon.

"We listen to each spacecraft every day," Voyager project manager Suzanne Dodd told Space.com at the event. Regarding Voyager 1, "we have an anomaly that is not something unseen before," Dodd said. "We do have a very strong carrier signal. We currently don't have a frame sync, so we're not getting the science or engineering data currently."

"But if you recall," Dodd added, "one light-day is very far from us. [It takes] 24 hours to get the signal to the spacecraft, 24 hours for the signal to come down, so it takes two days to diagnose anything on the spacecraft … So we can't do anything fast, and the data rates are very low."

According to Dodd, this anomaly isn't a major cause for long-term concern. Rather, it's likely just part of the natural aging process for spacecraft.

"These spacecraft are old," she said. "All of us old people have our aches and pains, and you know, sometimes it takes longer to stretch out your muscles and remember things, and so we are in a realm where we're going to see more anomalies with Voyager just due to the distance and the age."

"But we feel Voyager 1, although it's not sending us the telemetry data, it's pointed at the Earth. It's sending us a strong carrier signal, and I'm confident that it won't be too long, and it will send us data back."

And, for anyone curious how Voyager 1's slower twin Voyager 2 is faring, Dodd shared that it is doing very well on its own journey through interstellar space. "Voyager 2 is very healthy. It's continuing its mission. It has three scientific instruments on it," she told Space.com.

"Each spacecraft is basically setting a record every day for longevity or distance," Dodd added about the twin probes.

Despite the communications anomaly — and even though most of the team had been up all night to confirm the one-light-day signal from Voyager 1 — the room was alive with excitement. The Voyagers have been flying for nearly 50 years on a mission expected to last just five years, so the team was beyond ready to celebrate.

Amidst the more technical updates about the mission and its current status out in interstellar space, the event included footage of Voyager light-day celebrations from around the world. From children cheering on Voyager to iconic landmarks lighting up gold in honor of the mission's Golden Record, the Voyager love echoed around the world in photos and videos. The footage of this international celebration was enough to move Nicky Fox, NASA's associate administrator for the Science Mission Directorate, nearly to tears.

"I actually thought I was going to start sobbing," Fox joked to Space.com after the media briefing. "I think just hearing children's voices and people saying 'We love you, Voyager,' 'Go Voyager' — it just was amazing."

And Fox and the mission team members weren't the only ones sharing their love for Voyager. NASA astronaut Jessica Watkins beamed down her own video message to the Voyager 1 spacecraft from the International Space Station, sharing in the celebration.

"Dear Voyager 1," Watkins said. "As an orbiting outpost just 250 miles [400 kilometers] above Earth, we often feel distant yet deeply connected to our home planet. The International Space Station has had crew continuously living off of the Earth for 26 years, seen hundreds of visitors from around the world, and enabled thousands of experiments to advance space exploration.

"While you have been traveling into the unknown for nearly half a century, helping pave our path, together, our two journeys tell a single story of exploration," she continued. "One remains close to home, and the other reaches ever outward. Voyager 1, you have been a beacon of light, a symbol of exploration, a seeker of truth, a carrier of hope, a brave explorer, traveling further than any human hand has ever reached. You represent the very best of what humanity has to offer.

"As you reach a distance of one light-day from Earth, the entire crew of Expedition 75 and people across the planet send you our admiration and our wishes for a safe and continued journey as you begin your post-one-light-day era. There are so many amazing milestones and discoveries to come. You have a universe ahead of you, and you carry with you our collective hopes and dreams. When we look up from Earth, we will always think of you as you continue forward into the great unknown. May you continue shining bright for all of us. Go Voyager 1!" Watkins said, ending her message with a weightless backflip from aboard the station.

In concluding the media briefing, the mission team invited those in the room who, over the past 50 years (or more, with one person sharing their excitement having worked on Voyager before it was even called Voyager) have worked on the mission in one way or another, to stand up to a round of applause. With mission team members today ranging from those in their 20s to their 90s, the people behind this enduring mission span many generations.

Interestingly, Fox actually began her light-day celebrations earlier in the week. She, alongside writer/producer Ann Druyan — who was the wife of the late astronomer Carl Sagan, whose work helped create the mission's Golden Record and who took the now-famous "pale blue dot" Voyager image of Earth to the public — together lit up the Empire State Building gold.

"I was actually very privileged to flick the switch and turn on the Empire State Building lights on Tuesday evening [Nov. 17]," Fox, wearing golden Voyager-inspired nail polish crafted by her daughter, told Space.com. "That was just spectacular … I was there with Ann … we were both so giddy with the excitement of doing it … It was amazing. And then going up and standing underneath and seeing the tower was just really, really special."

While the room lit up with excitement for Voyager's light-day milestone on Thursday, the team was also brimming with anticipation for what lies ahead. As Voyager hurtles toward another major milestone, turning 50 in 2027, it remains an active science mission, and the team remains hard at work to push the boundaries of exploration with these aging probes.

"Voyager right now — the two Voyagers — they're in an area of space that we've never been into. So every single bit of data that comes back is precious because it's unique," Fox said.

"Now we're in a region where the sun's influence is a lot different, and we're in this region where we can start to understand: What is it like out past our sun? What is it like in the space between the stars?" Voyager deputy project scientist Jamie Rankin told Space.com. "We're at a very exciting time of starting to connect what we know and what we've seen, living with this star [our sun] to what is out there."

"What's next? Voyager is just going to keep on going. You know, people sometimes think, 'When's the mission going to end?' It's never going to end," Fox added to Space.com. "Even when we can no longer talk to Voyager, they're both going to keep going … So there's no end for the Voyager mission."

Chelsea Gohd served as a Senior Writer for Space.com from 2018 to 2022 before returning in 2026, covering everything from climate change to planetary science and human spaceflight in both articles and on-camera in videos. With a M.S. in Biology, Chelsea has written and worked for institutions including NASA JPL, the American Museum of Natural History, Scientific American, Discover Magazine Blog, Astronomy Magazine, and Live Science. Google adds computer use capabilities to Gemini’s Mac app

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Google LLC today released a new version of Gemini’s Mac client that can interact with other apps on the user’s computer.

The feature is available in the U.S. on launch. It requires a Google AI subscription, which equips consumer Google accounts with a broader lineup of artificial intelligence tools. The offering also includes additional storage space and certain other features.

Users can instruct Gemini to launch a local app by including the command “@Computer use” in their prompts. For example, a designer could upload a sketch of a vehicle and ask the AI assistant to turn it into a three-dimensional animation. Gemini finds the local app that is needed for the task, creates a project plan and carries out the work step by step.

The assistant supports tasks that comprise dozens of actions. Furthermore, consumers can use the output of a newly completed Gemini workflow to launch follow-up projects. A user could have the assistant generate a visual asset in a design tool and then turn it into a mini-app with Visual Studio Code.

Gemini can interact with not only local apps but also web services. That means it can load data from webpages into programs that run on the user’s machine, as well as move data in the opposite direction and between cloud services.

The AI carries out its work in a background window. As a result, users don’t have to pause their work while the assistant is running. They can track Gemini workflows via a preview pane built into the Mac client’s chat window. Additionally, Google has included controls that make it possible to give Gemini feedback mid-task.

“Privacy and safety are built in: you choose which apps Gemini can access, watch every step live, pause at any time, and approve key actions before they happen,” Michael Friedman, Google’s group product manager for the Gemini app, wrote in a blog post.

The Mac version of Muse, Meta Platforms Inc.’s recently launched AI agent, can also interact with local apps and data. Furthermore, a cloud-based virtual machine enables it to perform tasks even when the user’s computer is offline. Dots, a competing service from OpenAI Group PBC, offers a similar capability.

Google may seek to match its rivals’ cloud-based workflow hosting features in the future. The company can also extend Gemini in other ways. Notably, it could bring Gemini’s computer use mode to additional operating systems and add features for business users. OpenAI’s Dots already includes capabilities designed to automate business workflows such as procurement.

Jumia: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jumia is betting on operational improvements, including AI automation, and improved monetization to reaccelerate growth rather than relying on discounts to attract customers.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 19, 2026 at 6:41PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Jumia Technologies Ag ›

On its third quarter of 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 18, 2026, Jumia (JMIA -8.76%) announced that its adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) loss narrowed 43% year over year to $7.9 million. The e-commerce operator is proving its monetization strategy can shrink losses even as high memory chip costs hurt smartphone supply across Africa. Long-term gains depend on whether management can reignite customer growth without burning cash.

Three takeaways from the call reveal where the business is headed.

Global memory chip price spikes have driven up prices and caused shortages for smartphones and other electronics across key markets. Smartphones serve as the main gateway for new shoppers; hardware price inflation is dampening broader engagement.

Jumia is increasing its gross margins by cutting costs and improving monetization. Management isn't buying top-line growth through heavy promotional discounts. Instead, higher commission rates and expanding retail advertising services are pushing gross margins higher while keeping fixed overhead lean.

External factors, including smartphone supply and cocoa prices, have put pressure on customers. Jumia's management believes its value offering will help it gain market share. The strategy relies on removing user friction and improving software conversion tools rather than increasing marketing spend.

Jumia updated its full-year 2026 guidance because its early Black Friday trends were below expectations, particularly in the Ivory Coast. Management is using headcount reductions and artificial intelligence tools to trim operational expenses. It is improving its pickup station logistics network, expanding international merchant sourcing, and maintaining strict fee discipline to protect unit margins.

If logistics expansion and social affiliate tools reignite order volume, the enterprise can compound value on a leaner overhead structure.

Copa Holdings (CPA) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Management pushes fleet-wide Starlink rollout and a Panama hub expansion ahead.

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Updated Nov 19, 2026 at 6:41PM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Copa Holdings (CPA +5.52%) reported third-quarter financial results that reflected a focus on the Panama hub and a structurally low unit cost base. Management indicated that demand trends remained strong across all operating regions, supporting 16.3% capacity growth while maintaining a load factor of 88.7%. The company stated its intention to increase connectivity by expanding from six to eight connecting banks in Panama starting in March of next year and continues the implementation of satellite-based internet across its fleet. Financial results were supported by the recovery of 86% of higher fuel costs through increased passenger yields and unit revenues.

Operator: Ladies and gentlemen, thank you for standing by. Welcome to Copa Holdings Third Quarter Earnings Call. During the presentation, all participants will be in listen only mode. Afterwards, we will conduct a question and answer session. At that time, if you have a question, you will have to press star-1-1 on your touch tone phone. As a reminder, this call is being webcast and recorded on 11/19/2026. Now I will turn the conference call over to Daniel Tapia, Director of Investor Relations. Sir, you may begin.

Daniel Tapia: Thank you, Marvin, and welcome, everyone, to our third quarter earnings call. Joining me today are Mr. Pedro Heilbron, Executive Chairman and CEO of Copa Holdings, Mr. Robert Carey, Executive Vice President and Mr. Peter Donkersloot Ponce. Our CFO. Peter will begin with an overview of the quarter and go through our strategic priorities. Robert will then discuss our operating and commercial highlights. Peter will then conclude by going through our financial results and outlook. Immediately after, we will open the call for questions from analysts. In the 2.2% year over year and delivered an operating margin of 18.6%. In spite of much higher fuel costs. These results reflect the strength of Copa's business model. Built on Panama's strategic geographic location, the connectivity and convenience of our Hub of the Americas, a structurally low unit cost base, and a passenger friendly product.

Together, these competitive advantages continue to differentiate Copa and support our ability to generate industry leading financial results over the long term. Demand environment remained robust in the quarter, resulting in higher unit revenues and a load factor of close to 89%. Even as we increase capacity by more than 16%. These results reinforce our confidence in the long term growth opportunities in The Americas and in our ability to continue generating profitable growth in the short and long term. Also remain focused on strengthening the competitiveness of our Hub of The Americas in Panama.

Transition from 6 to 8 connecting banks beginning in March of next year will further enhance connectivity, increase schedule options for our passengers, and improved air purification. At the same time, the rollout of STARLINK continues to advance further improving the customer experience and strengthening our value proposition. Robert Carey: Thank you, Pedro, and good morning, everyone. Let me begin by reviewing some of the quarter's key commercial highlights. Capacity measured in ASMs increased 16.3% compared to Q3 25. Load factor increased 0.7 percentage points to 88.7%. Passenger yields increased 13.8% year over year, and as a result, our operating revenues increased 31.9% year over year to $1.2 billion. Unit revenues or RASM increased 13.5% 12.6¢. These results reflect strong demand and pricing trends across our network, with both higher yields and higher load factors, allowing us to continue increasing unit revenues while growing capacity at a double digit pace.

Of course, we have also maintained our focus on costs and these results are delivered together with a continued competitive unit cost position. Looking ahead, booking trends continue to be strong, supporting our expectation of higher unit revenues than previously anticipated through the remainder of this year. Consistent with these trends, we have updated our 2026 outlook, which Peter will share later. Turning to our network. We remain on track to launch service to Porlamar, Venezuela next week. With this addition, Copa Airlines will serve 88 destinations in 32 countries across The Americas. Further strengthening the connectivity offered through our Hub of the Americas. On the operational side, Copa Airlines once again delivered industry leading reliability.

During the quarter, we achieved an on time performance of 87.1% and a completion factor of 99.7%. Positioning Copa among the most reliable airlines in the world. Regarding product, implementation of Starlink continues on track with approximately a quarter of our fleet currently equipped. We expect to have full implementation across the Copa fleet by mid 27. Regarding our fleet plan, we expect to take delivery of 1 additional Boeing 37 MAX 8 before the year end. Looking ahead to 2027, we currently receiving 12 Boeing 37 MAX 8 aircraft and retiring 2 Boeing 737-700 aircraft ending 2027 with a fleet of approximately 142 aircraft.

This outlook is based on our current order book and remains consistent with Copa's long term growth plan. Of course, as you are all aware, Boeing has announced they will maintain rate 47 for the first half of 27 creating risk of delivery delays. At this point, we have not received notification of any delays. Given our delivery schedule next year, our expectation is that while delays may impact the fleet count at year end, we do not currently anticipate significant impact to our capacity plans for the year. And with that, I will turn the call over to Peter, who will review our financial performance and outlook in greater detail.

Peter Donkersloot Ponce: Thank you, Robert, and good morning, everyone. Let me begin with our financial highlights. We reported a net profit of $176 million or $4.29 per share. Representing a 2.2% increase in earnings per share compared to the third quarter of 2025. Operating income totaled $224 million an increase of 5.7%. Compared to the third quarter of 2025. Resulting in an operating margin of 18.6%. Net margin for the quarter was 14.6%, a 4.4 percentage points decrease year over year. Despite all in average jet fuel price increasing 61.5% year over year, strong demand and higher yields enable us to recover approximately 86% of the increase in fuel-related expense in the quarter through higher revenues.

As a reminder, our fuel recovery calculation compares the year over year increase in revenues attributable to higher RASM with the year over year increase in fuel expenses resulting from higher all in jet fuel prices. Both calculated using 2026 capacity levels. Unit cost excluding fuel or ex fuel CASM increased 1.1%. Year over year to 5.7¢. The year over year comparison in xFuel CASM was impacted by a benefit recorded in the third quarter of 25 related to the extension of 1 leased aircraft. As well as an $8.3 million benefit recorded in the third quarter of 2026 related to an increase in the discount rate used to calculate the present value of expected end of lease cost.

Adjusting for both items, ex fuel CASM would have increased approximately 1.7% year over year, mainly driven by the appreciation of certain Latin American currencies. Turning now to our balance sheet and liquidity. We ended the quarter with $1.6 billion in cash, short term and long term investments, representing 38% of last 12 month revenue. Total debt, including these liabilities, stood at approximately $2.6 billion Our average cost of debt is currently 3.8%, with approximately half of it at fixed interest rates. We ended the quarter with a net debt to EBITDA ratio of 0.8 times, In addition, we continue to maintain substantial flexibility to our unencumbered asset base, which includes 1/3 of our fleet and 16 spare engines.

Turning now to shareholder returns. Our Board of Directors ratified the company's fourth quarterly dividend payment of $1.71 per share payable on December 15 to all shareholders of record as of November 30. Now, turning to our full year outlook. While demand trend across the network continues to be strong, fuel prices have increased relative to the prior outlook assumptions. And as such, we are now expected full year operating margin in the range of 16% to 17% with capacity growth of approximately 14.5%. This outlook assumes a load factor of 87.5 RASM in the range of 12.3 to 12.4¢, ex fuel CASM of 5.8¢, and an all in fuel price of $3.90 per gallon.

Our outlook also includes the estimated $12 million to $14 million impact of the earthquake related disruption at the Tocumen International Airport. Preliminary for 2027 currently anticipate capacity growth in the range of 8% to 9%. Based on our current order book projections and consistent with Copa's long term. We currently expect ex fuel CASM to be in the range of 5.7 to 5.8¢. To summarize, demand and revenue trends remain strong. We continue to maintain disciplined cost control, Our strong balance sheet remains a key competitive advantage. And our proven business model continues to position us well to navigate fuel price volatility while generating profitable growth and long term shareholder value.

Peter Donkersloot Ponce: Yes. Sure, Mike. So accounting standards in our case, IFRS requires to peg it in case of Panama. The Panama company to Panama bond. So when the interest rates increase, Panama bond yields increases, we use that number to update it and calculate the net present value. We have thresholds, so we do not update every quarter. We update it when it surpass certain thresholds. And that is what we have to do with this. So it is pegged to the Panama bond yield.

So for that reason, we feel that prices are going to be pretty sticky. Thank you.

Elon Musk says SpaceX has a plan to eliminate cell phone dead zones

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

CEO Elon Musk said SpaceX has completed the design of a miniature rooftop cell tower that could provide better coverage to mobile phones and eliminate dead zones inside buildings where signals from its Starlink satellites have trouble reaching.

These mini cell towers are expected to go into production in 2027, Musk said, during an interview at the DealBook Summit this week.

“We’ve got a plan that enables ubiquitous access on Earth, including via cell phone,” Musk said, noting the company is launching its Direct-to-Cell V2 satellite constellation next year, which will increase bandwidth 40-fold. “But there is a missing piece of the puzzle here which is that if you’re indoors, especially if you have a metal roof, that blocks the signal from space. So we need something that’s terrestrial that addresses situations where people are, you know, in a building with a thick roof essentially.”

The company’s efforts initially focused on buying wireless spectrum after receiving the Federal Communications Commission’s approval in 2024 to move forward with plans to offer a direct-to-phone version of its Starlink satellite internet service, in partnership with T-Mobile. SpaceX struck a $17 billion deal with EchoStar in 2025 and agreed this year to acquire a portfolio of low-band spectrum valued at $8 billion.

Combining that wireless spectrum with mini cell towers should boost signals and end the dead zone problem, he said. According to Musk, the company has completed the design of what he described as “a micro cell tower thing that you just put on your roof, like a Starlink dish.”

Musk, who has a history of missing his own production deadlines, demurred saying the devices were “going into production, I think, next year.”

The idea isn’t new, but it wasn’t clear until this week how much progress SpaceX had made toward developing the hardware.

SpaceX President Gwynne Shotwell said during the company’s August earnings call that it wouldn’t use big cell towers and would instead deploy small, low-power cellular base stations.

Even with this latest progress report, SpaceX appears to be still working out the details. Musk said one approach would be to bundle the small cellular base stations together with Starlink terminals.

SpaceX’s plan to build a hybrid celluar-satellite network has fueled speculation, and even predictions, that legacy telecom companies could be in danger. But Musk doesn’t think so because he expects an entirely new class of customers to drive demand for wireless bandwidth: AI.

“I don’t think this means, like you know, AT&T, Verizon, or others go out of business or anything,” he said, adding that maybe they’ll lose some revenue. “But I actually think that the demand for bandwidth will be tremendous in the future, and a lot, and that bandwidth will be driven primarily by superintelligence and robots, and they’re going to need a lot more bandwidth than humans.”

Anthropic becomes one of world's most valuable public companies in stock market debut

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Anthropic has made one of the fastest trips from start up to IPO in history

Anthropic has become one of the most valuable public companies in the world after its shares began trading on the stock market, taking its valuation to roughly $1.76 trillion (Â£1.34 trillion).

The artificial intelligence (AI) company's Thursday debut on the Nasdaq stock exchange was a crucial test for the industry, given the huge amount of money being poured into advancing the technology.

Anthropic's shares flew past its allocation price of $83 each, showing there was plenty of excitement around AI.

Yet the company, which is behind AI chatbot Claude, will remain under intense scrutiny in the months ahead to answer the key question surrounding AI: is it all worth it?

In just a few hours of trading, Anthropic's stock seemed to show that investors remain uncertain of where exactly AI is heading. The share price rose immediately to $111.53, sending the company's market value to almost $2 trillion, before falling by the end of the day, with shares closing at $97.17.

Some investors said Anthropic's debut seemed to be a success, as shares rose roughly 17% during the day, but the broader tech market did not perform as well.

The tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite Index closed down nearly 2.3%. Stocks fell in the largest tech firms, including Google's parent company Alphabet, which was down by 2.9%, while Amazon lost almost 3.9%.

The buildout happening around this era of AI is among the most expensive endeavours in the history of technology, with more than $1 trillion being spent in the last year alone by a handful of major tech companies.

The speed at which investment has grown has led to ongoing concerns of a financial bubble.

"Bubbles don't burst on the day of the largest deal," Richard Whittle, an economist with the University of Salford said. "They burst later, if the positive future becomes suspect."

Simon Jack: Can Anthropic's record-breaking IPO stop the AI bubble bursting?

While the basics of the technology have been around for decades, the more recent advent of more powerful computers and related components have allowed the processing of more data than ever.

The result has been AI chatbots that simulate human speech and operate more quickly tasks like software coding, digital image generation, and searching the internet.

It represents a new way of using computers that has led to the rapid rise of Anthropic, which started just five years ago and is best known as the maker of the popular AI chatbot Claude.

As the first purely AI company to go public, Anthropic will almost certainly see significant swings in price on the stock market going forward, according to Ben Barringer, who leads technology research at Quilter Cheviot.

The "initial exuberance" will wear off, Barringer said, pointing to what happened with SpaceX earlier this year.

That company, led by Elon Musk, saw shares jump by about 10% on the first day of trading, taking the company to a record-breaking valuation of $2.2 trillion.

Within a month shares had fallen below their debut price, and have mostly fallen further since.

Most tech companies see their shares fall after hitting the stock market, often after reporting quarterly results for the first time.

But many such companies, including Meta, Amazon and Google, have gone on to become the largest and most profitable corporations in the world.

With Anthropic, investors are not only looking at a notable tech company with a major valuation, and a potentially big future in business, but a company that is the first of its kind on the stock market.

"There's no obvious peer to compare it to," Neil Wilson, an investor strategist at Saxo UK, said.

So, as individual investors and even researchers and investors at large funds ask themselves if Anthropic is really worth more than $1.7 trillion, or even more, there will be few answers available.

"We'll have a stab at it," Wilson said. "I don't think anyone really knows."

A few things will likely impact the company's success on the stock market in the near-term.

One of those things is the prospect of new laws governing the use of AI tools. In the US, where Anthropic is based, Democrats will be in many more positions of power come 2027, and several politicians have already said they will be looking to ensure AI tools are safe and have some guardrails attached.

Such regulation is currently unlikely at the federal level. US President Donald Trump on Thursday spoke about AI during the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation summit in China, alongside Chinese and Russian counterparts, Xi Jinping and Vladimir Putin.

Trump reiterated his position against "any form of robust regulation" around the technology, adding, "we won't allow that to happen, frankly."

The same day, however, a bipartisan group of US lawmakers formed the House AI Safety and Innovation Caucus, which will work specifically on AI regulation and safety considerations.

A significant driver of this kind of scrutiny is Anthropic itself. Company executives, including co-founder and CEO Dario Amodei, have been outspoken in their purported fears that future AI tools will become so capable they will outsmart humans, to the point where extinction is possible.

Anthropic has always put itself forward in public as an AI company that takes the safety of its technology very seriously.

Daniela Amodei, Anthropic's president and sister of Dario Amodei, boasted on Thursday that the company "had an AI safety team before we launched our first product".

Despite the company's claims that the technology it is building at a rapid pace could be dangerous, Anthropic has made one of the quickest trips from start-up to IPO in history.

Anthropic raises $81bn in record-breaking stock market debut

Despite posting a solid earnings beat, Elastic sees its stock slide on rough guidance

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Elastic N.V.’s stock was trending lower in late trading today after the enterprise search software firm reported earnings and revenue that beat Wall Street’s expectations but disappointed with guidance that came in below forecasts.

The company reported second-quarter earnings before certain costs such as stock compensation of 94 cents per share, easily beating the analyst consensus estimate of 81 cents. Revenue rose 17%, to $496 million, surpassing the Street’s target of $487 million.

The results helped Elastic bounce back into profitability. Its net income for the quarter came to $171 million, rebounding from a loss of $51.3 million in the same period one year ago.

However, investors were less than impressed with the company’s guidance. For the quarter in progress, Elastic said it’s looking for earnings of between 90 cents and 92 cents per share on sales of $507.5 million to $508.5 million. That’s some way below the Street’s targets of 95 cents in earnings and $517.6 million in revenue. The company is not helped by the weak Euro, with currency conversions likely to cause a $4.5 million headwind on revenue in the quarter.

The full-year picture is not much better, as Elastic forecast annual sales of between $2 billion and $2.01 billion compared with the Street’s model of $2.01 billion.

Netherlands-based Elastic is one of Europe’s most successful software companies. It’s the developer of the popular open-source enterprise search platform Elasticsearch, which is used by thousands of businesses all over the world. The base platform is free to download and use, with Elastic making money by selling a special cloud-hosted version that ships with premium features not available in the open edition. The company also sells application observability and security tools.

In the last couple of years, Elastic has tried to cash in on the artificial intelligence boom by positioning Elasticsearch as a useful cog in the wheel that feeds real-time data into large language models to provide them with business context. A key capability is Elastic Context Engine, which gathers, structures, and serves business-specific context to AI agents so they can make accurate decisions with fewer tokens and lower latency.

More recently, the company has built AI-native products including AlertZero, which enables enterprises to orchestrate teams of autonomous security agents that automate tasks around data protection and threat hunting.

Elastic Chief Executive Ash Kulkarni (pictured) said he’s excited about the potential of those new capabilities to drive significant revenue over time. “Elastic delivered another strong quarter, beating guidance across all key metrics,” he said. “Our strong results reflect expanding customer adoption and position us well to continue our strong execution in the back half of fiscal 2027.”

Unfortunately for Kulkarni, investors didn’t seem to share his enthusiasm. The market was clearly expecting trouble on the horizon in the hours before the company published its report, as its stock declined 8% during regular trading. Once the results were out, it fell another 8% after-hours. That means Elastic’s stock is now up just over 1% in the year to date.

Elastic’s stock had rallied more than 150% between April and October, but virtually all of those gains have now been erased. Prior to today, the shares had already lost more than 20% this month amid broader concerns about the AI industry. Some investors continue to worry about the potential of AI technology to challenge the business models of software companies.

For the last decade, Ben and Erin Napier have been renovating homes in the small Southern town of Laurel, Mississippi, on HGTV’s wildly popular show Home Town. The series’ 10th season premiered in January on the 2026 TV schedule, and fans, including me, have been itching for any sort of update on the famous reality couple. In particular, I’ve been waiting for an update on Season 11, and Erin finally spoke out.

Season 10 wrapped up in March, and Erin revealed in August that filming had just started back up on the new episodes. While there isn’t any information on when to expect Home Town''s exact return, there is one big piece of news that will surely make people happy: Erin took to her social channels earlier this week to share the episodes are just about done, and she even posted photos from the wrap party:

A post shared by Erin Napier (@erinapier)

Considering it’s been a trying year for the Napiers, it’s great to see them finally able to celebrate something. Last December, the hotel they were renovating for their series, Home Town: Inn This Together, caught on fire, close to Erin’s 40th birthday.

Fans were able to see the hard work the couple and their team have put into the hotel for the spinoff, which ended with the hotel fire (streaming now with an HBO Max subscription), but I love that they can look forward to the future of Home Town, which is certainly not slowing down any time soon thanks to the Napiers and their incredible team:

Season 10 will be all buttoned up in a couple of weeks but last night we celebrated. You all must know that there’s no way one person can manage the design processes of 9 houses at the same time, and so I get to lean on this incredible team. Together we make a plan then everyone goes on whatever journey the house gives them, shopping and making every design choice with me. They are the many eyes and hands and feet that get to these houses across the finish line and I get to be an editor in chief of sorts, and it feels like magic every time.

Welp, I didn't see this confusion coming. I’m excited to see what kind of houses the Napiers are renovating in the new season, which is billed as Season 10, but is actually Season 11. The Season 10 finale aired in March, so these new batch of episodes, coming sometime in 2027, will certainly be Season 11.

It's a little confusing, and the Home Town stars know it. Erin clarified why she referred to the new episodes as Season 10, rather than Season 11, when asked by a fan, and her response makes a lot of sense:

So, because of a programming discrepancy like 4 years ago, the numbers are off! y'all will know Season 10 as Season 11.

It’s definitely confusing, but I'm glad it was addressed. There are plenty of shows whose production numbers don’t line up with episode numbers, for whatever reason. For instance, a big Jeopardy! inconsistency Ken Jennings revealed last year was that the production numbers currently being used don’t equal the total number of episodes produced, all because of mistakes from decades ago.

Regardless, I’m relieved to know production is just about wrapped, meaning we’ll be getting the Napiers back on our TVs soon. It's also worth noting, new seasons of Home Town typically premiere in December or January, and since the new season is still being worked on for the next couple of weeks, if the pattern sticks, we could be looking the same timeline for Season 10 (11). For now, the wait continues!

The GTA 6 star system says a lot more than just your Wanted level

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Rockstar Games' first gameplay trailer for Grand Theft Auto 6 from August suggested the developer had tinkered with some of the core mechanics of the series, including Wanted levels. Now that GTA 6 is out, we have a much better sense of how much has actually changed. While your Wanted level has always been able to communicate a sense of how many police officers are looking for you and how intently they're looking, the updated system also bakes in a lot more detail about what police actively know about protagonists Jason and Lucia.

Some of this is thanks to the game's new concept of Heat, pieces of information the police learn about the player characters that can linger even after your Wanted level lowers. But the visual changes to the Wanted system's iconic star ratings convey nearly as much. Here's how Rockstar's approach to Wanted levels has changed in Grand Theft Auto VI.

The most obvious visual difference between GTA 6 and GTA 5 is the reintroduction of a sixth Wanted level. In the last Grand Theft Auto, your Wanted level maxed out at five stars. As Rockstar teased during its gameplay trailer, GTA 6 caps out at six stars. This is a return to the rating system of older games, going as far back as Grand Theft Auto III — or even further if you consider the "police heads" top-down GTA games used to communicate police response.

As in past games, the number of stars lets you know how hard the police are looking for you. One star could just be a few cops tailing you, while a full six stars indicates a dramatic, militarized response. Besides being a throwback, opting for six stars seems like it allows Rockstar to offer some nuance and variety to the series' classic chase experience. You might eventually get bored if every bank robbery devolves into a helicopter chase.

Just committing a robbery doesn't necessarily mean your Wanted level will rise, either. According to IGN's hands-on demo and interview with Rockstar North co-studio head Rob Nelson, crimes need to actually be reported to police to provoke a response. "The Wanted system in GTA 6 requires that someone witness the crime, or an alarm needs to be set off for it to be reported to the law," Nelson told IGN. "Our dispatch is extremely specific in what it knows."

Once you are "wanted" for a crime, GTA 6 offers a lot more visual information to tell you what police currently know and nudge you toward escaping their scrutiny. Those include icons for the different kinds of information the police know about you as part of the Heat system, and also different treatments of your Wanted level star rating.

When police have direct line of sight with you, your stars are solid white, according to GameSpot, and only start flashing white once you've broken line of sight. Meanwhile, your stars turn red when you're near a crime scene but the police don't know where you are or hollow white when the police are looking for you but have no idea what you look like.

At the same time, some information about the police is also obstructed. GameSpot reports you won't be able to see the police on your minimap, which makes it harder to plan escape routes in advance. It also says once you do reach a six-star Wanted level, you'll be pursued throughout the game map, making clean exits more difficult. All of these changes seem like they'll make the fundamental cops-and-robbers gameplay of Grand Theft Auto 6 more engaging. Or at the very least, different from what people have come to expect from the series.

If you’ve been eyeballing a Kindle Colorsoft, now’s your chance to snag 47% off

/C O R R E C T I O N - Chargeflow/

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In the news release, 80% of Merchants Use AI for Chargebacks, but Only 11% Have Automated, Chargeflow and Juniper Research Finds, issued 17-Nov-2026 by Chargeflow over PR Newswire, we are advised by the company that changes have been made. The complete, corrected release follows, with additional details at the end:

Merchants who use AI report a chargeback dispute median win rate of 38%, up from 27.75%

NEW YORK, Nov. 17, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Chargeflow, the AI chargeback platform used by more than 20,000 merchants, today published "The State of Chargeback Management: How AI Is Reshaping Performance," a survey of 121 enterprise merchants in the U.S. and U.K. conducted by Juniper Research. Merchants that use AI win a median 38% of the chargebacks they dispute, against 27.75% for those that don't. Yet the median merchant in both groups disputes and wins the same share of its eligible chargebacks: 6.65%.

AI users also contest the same median share of their eligible chargebacks as non-users. Juniper Research points to a gap between adopting AI and changing how the work gets done: 80% of respondents use AI in chargeback management, but only 11% have mostly or fully automated it.

The report's findings come as pressure on merchants grows. Chargebacks rose over the past 18 months for 65% of respondents, and nine in 10 of those saw growth of 10% or more. Almost half put the annual cost of chargebacks at 0.5% to 1% of revenue. Friendly fraud is a large part of the problem: 43% believe it accounts for more than a quarter of their chargebacks.

"Most merchants have added AI to a manual process," said Dan Moshkovich, VP of Go-to-Market at Chargeflow. "It helps them win more of the disputes they file, but people still decide which ones to file, so they fight the same small share. That's the next step for AI in chargebacks: knowing which disputes are worth fighting and building a case for each one."

Merchants want evidence of higher win rates to justify AI investment

Asked what would justify spending on AI for chargebacks, 39% of merchants chose a higher win rate and 34% chose more revenue recovered. Fewer than one in five chose time saved or lower costs. Most want a big gain before they buy: 69% said a tool would need to lift their win rate by at least 11 percentage points, taking a merchant that wins 30% of its disputes to 41% or more. AI hasn't cleared that bar yet: AI users report a median win rate 10.25 percentage points higher than non-users, just short of the 11-point mark. Juniper Research concludes that "while AI has delivered incremental improvements, it is not yet enough to meet enterprise expectations." When choosing a provider, 72% rated the quality of its data as very important and 70% said the same of security and compliance, against 31% for total cost.

Of respondents, 77% said chargebacks rise over the December holidays. Most are still optimistic: 58% expect lower chargeback losses in the 2026 season than in 2025. Chargeflow's own data shows what changes over that peak. The Chargeflow Data Lab, launched Oct. 22, studied about 3 million disputes across the last three peak seasons. Over the 2025 Black Friday to Cyber Monday weekend, 34.7% of chargebacks said the order never arrived, up from 19.9% in a normal week. Fraud claims fell to 27.2% from 37.8%, and the same shift appeared in 2023, 2024 and 2025.

As AI matures, chargeback tools will be judged on whether they turn intelligence into better decisions and measurable financial gains," said Nick Maynard, VP of Research, Juniper Research. "The opportunity is not simply to automate chargeback management. It is to make chargeback management materially more effective."

Juniper Research surveyed 121 C-level executives and senior managers at U.S. and U.K. businesses with at least $100 million in annual gross merchandise value in September 2026: 80 in the U.S. and 41 in the U.K. The full report is available at chargeflow.io/data-lab/the-state-of-chargeback-management.

Chargeflow is the industry-leading AI Chargeback Platform, with more than $50 billion in transactions protected. It operates on a simple belief: every business has the right to keep the revenue it has earned.

Many businesses write off chargebacks as a cost of doing business. Chargeflow fights for that revenue instead. Its data-driven AI catches fraudulent orders before fulfillment, resolves eligible disputes before they become chargebacks, and builds the strongest case on the chargebacks that get through. Representment comes with no upfront cost and no financial commitment: merchants pay only when Chargeflow wins.

Trusted by more than 20,000 merchants and more than 300 enterprise customers, Chargeflow is the No. 1-ranked provider on G2 for both chargeback management and e-commerce fraud protection.

Internet connection speeds are consistently rising most everywhere in the world, and more than a few spots already have multi-gigabit service available. Making those kinds of speeds available throughout your house, however, requires a competent router that can handle both the high throughput and a fairly high number of connected devices. Enter the TP-Link Archer BE9500 Wi-Fi 7 router with 10 Gbps WAN, which, as of right now, is on sale at Best Buy for under a single Benjamin, at $89.99 — or under half its regular price of $199.99.

TP-Link networking gear has earned a good reputation with techies thanks to good reliability and offering great specs at quite affordable pricing, as is the case in point. Unlike some routers that outright lie about supporting WiFi 7, the Archer BE9500 actually has the magical combo that fully enables the power of the latest protocol: a 6 GHz band, along with Multi Link Operation (MLO) support.

The TP-Link Archer BE9500 is a full-featured Wi-Fi 7 router with full 6 GHz and MLO support and a 10 Gbps WAN port for high-speed internet connections.

That grants the BE9500 an entire 9.5 Gbps of combined wireless bandwidth across all three frequencies, broadcast into the airwaves by six beam-forming antennas. For physical connections, there's one 10 Gbps WAN port, along with two 2.5 Gbps connectors for your big workstations or gaming rigs, and an additional two gigabit ports.

As befits a modern router, the Archer BE9500 supports EasyMesh for wiring additional devices, as well as separate guest and internal networks for segregating IoT and other classes of devices. There are four VPN flavors on offer for acting as both a server and a client, including support for the modern WireGuard protocol.

A USB port for connecting storage media, parental controls, automatic firmware upgrade, VLAN tagging, and miscellaneous features round out the main features. Get this router while stocks last, for only $89.99 at Best Buy.

Robert Glasper breaks down why AI can’t copy the human “mistakes” that make music feel real.

In this Billboard News interview, Glasper talks bridging jazz, hip-hop, R&B and soul — and what separates great musicians from artists who change culture. Glasper also reflects on working with Kendrick Lamar on To Pimp a Butterfly, why H.E.R. excites him, why so much modern R&B can sound the same, and why the industry often values what’s easiest to market over musicianship.

AI can’t copy this. I’m Robert Glasper. AI can’t copy- AI can’t copy this! You want some of this, AI? Huh? AI ain’t got nothing on me!

So you’ve spent your career bridging jazz, hip-hop, R&B and soul all into the same conversation.

When you look at music today, do you feel like the industry has finally caught up to the sound that you’ve been championing, or it’s still a long way to go?

I feel like it’s catching up. You know? We’re not all the way there, but I think that door is open to where people are more open to exploring different genres of music and artists not feeling like they have to be a monolithic thing. I always say ‘go room to room in the house of Black music.’

And feel free and open to do that, ’cause so many artists are influenced by more than one genre of music.

So far, you collaborated with so many people, right?

On top of mind, Kendrick Lamar, Erykah Badu. What do you think separates a great musician from somebody who actually changes the direction of music?

Great musicians are great with fitting into what was already there. They don’t reinvent the machine, but they just make sure the machine’s oiled and works. Then there are certain people who come around that totally change your perception of whatever, whatever that is. Erykah and, and Kendrick are people who have done that. You know?

Resolve AI’s SRE-1 model finds root causes of production alerts as AI agents go on call

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Incident remediation company Resolve AI Inc. has announced the launch of SRE-1, a new model that has been trained to find the root cause of production alerts, and Primary On-call, a new service that puts its artificial intelligence agents on call ahead of human engineers.

The new model, announced Wednesday, reflects the company’s argument that while AI has transformed how software gets written, it has barely touched how software gets run. According to Resolve AI, production has no public training corpus to match the published code that models learned from, as incidents at each company stay private.

SRE-1 is a 27 billion-parameter model built on open weights and trained by a team of 16 AI researchers over a final run of 7.4 days on up to 512 graphics processing units. Measured on the same incidents before and after training, which used reinforcement learning alone, the pass rate for a single investigation rose from 6.9% to 38.8%.

Resolve AI used Snowflake Inc.’s Cortex Training, a serverless service for customizing open-weight models, to build SRE-1. Snowflake Senior Vice President of Engineering Vivek Raghunathan said Resolve AI was the first design partner for the service.

In testing on 49 difficult live production cases, each run five times, an ensemble of five SRE-1 investigations matched the strongest frontier model tested at a lower cost, according to the company. Resolve AI also benchmarked its complete system on 178 production alert investigations, solving about twice as many incidents as general-purpose coding agents.

Primary On-call puts that research to work on the critical path. Each page is investigated across telemetry, infrastructure, code and operational knowledge, according to the company. Depending on what it finds, the agent can close the alert, mitigate the problem where a team’s policies permit or escalate to a human. Once it has acted, the agent checks the fix against the production baseline, monitoring it until recovery holds.

The agent also links related alerts and investigations into a shared view of each underlying problem. For teams wary of handing over the pager straight away, Resolve AI offers a shadow mode that records the call the agent would have made on each page.

Up to 80% of paged alerts in production are said to be handled by Primary On-call without escalation. Customers have reported a tenfold improvement in mean time to resolution, along with root-cause accuracy of up to 95%, according to Resolve AI.

Salesforce Inc. is among the early users. Bhupesh Patel, a software engineering principal architect at Salesforce, said that in the first few weeks of dark launch testing, the agent closed more than 70% of the CRM Analytics team’s paging alerts “without waking an engineer.”

“No engineering team can keep up with how fast code and patches land in production,” said Resolve AI Chief Executive Spiros Xanthos. “The answer is agents that are accurate and fast enough to sit on the critical path, and that takes a model built for production.”

Primary On-call is now generally available. Access to SRE-1 is limited to Resolve AI’s own platform.

Resolve AI is a venture capital-backed startup that has raised more than $190 million to date, including a $40 million extension to its Series A round in April, led by DST Global and Salesforce Ventures, that valued the company at $1.5 billion. Notable customers include Coinbase Global Inc., DoorDash Inc., Figma Inc., MSCI Inc., Nasdaq Inc., SAP SE and Zscaler Inc.

A Bitcoin trade that doesn’t need prices to rise could beat Treasury bills in 2027

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sygnum expects Bitcoin carry trades to beat Treasury bills in 2027 as funding rates and futures premiums recover.

Sygnum expects crypto carry trades to earn more than Treasury bills on average in 2027, according to its annual Sygnal outlook released Nov. 19.

The bank also pointed to a recovery in Bitcoin funding rates and futures premiums, citing 67 consecutive days of negative Bitcoin funding in 2026.

The bank expects renewed demand for leverage to keep funding rates and futures basis mostly positive in 2027, even after the Federal Reserve raised its policy-rate target range to 3.75%-4% in September.

The trade pairs a Bitcoin purchase with a short futures position. If the two positions are sized to offset each other, a rise in Bitcoin helps the purchase and hurts the short. The trader seeks to earn a premium on dated futures or collect funding payments on perpetual futures.

With a dated futures contract, the gap between the futures price and the spot price is called the basis. When the contract trades above spot, the trader can buy Bitcoin at the lower price and sell futures at the higher price, and the two prices converge at expiry.

Perpetual futures have no expiry, so their funding payments recur while positions are open. When funding is positive, longs pay shorts, and shorts pay longs when it is negative. The 67-day streak Sygnum cites describes how long funding stayed negative, when traders shorting perpetuals would have faced a funding cost.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Buy Bitcoin and short futures. The source of the return depends on the type of contract.

Dated futures seek to capture an initial price premium. Perpetual futures pay or charge funding while the trade is open.

Trading and financing costs affect realized returns. Sygnum’s release gives no bill maturity or yield for the comparison.

Conceptual diagram of the carry structure described in Sygnum’s outlook. The dated futures lines represent a positive initial futures basis converging at expiry. Arrows show the direction of payments under positive and negative perpetual funding.

Trading and financing costs affect realized returns. Sygnum’s release gives no bill maturity or yield for the comparison.

The Treasury-bill comparison asks what the same capital could earn in short-term US government debt. The Fed’s policy rate sets the context for short-term rates.

In Sygnal Six, the bank estimates that if about $360 billion, or 0.75% of Nasdaq market value, moved into crypto, total crypto market capitalization could rise more than 60% and Bitcoin could reach a new high near $128,000.

Those two forecasts sit alongside six others on token value accrual, Bitcoin in strategic reserves, stablecoins in correspondent banking, tokenization, prediction markets and AI demand.

Fabian Dori, the bank’s chief investment officer and the report’s lead author, said investors should look beyond price to fundamentals.

Mostly positive funding rates and futures premiums in 2027 would support Sygnum’s case for a carry recovery. The test is whether the resulting average return clears Treasury bills.

RADIN Health Unveils RADINA™, the Autonomous AI Ecosystem for Imaging, at RSNA 2026

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

One autonomous AI platform guiding every patient from referral to follow-up, automating repetitive work, connecting workflows, and helping imaging organizations scale without adding administrative complexity.

SHERIDAN, Wyo., Nov. 19, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- RADIN Health today announced RADINA, a next-generation AI platform designed to automate and orchestrate imaging workflows across the entire patient journey. Scheduled to be officially unveiled at RSNA 2026, RADINA serves as the AI layer behind RADINâ¢, connecting operational, clinical, and patient-facing workflows into a single intelligent ecosystem.

Imaging organizations face continued pressure from workforce shortages, rising labor costs, increasing patient volumes, reimbursement cuts, and growing compliance requirements. While healthcare software has digitized many imaging processes, organizations still rely heavily on staff to coordinate referrals, insurance verification, prior authorizations, scheduling, patient communications, reporting workflows, and follow-up management.

"Healthcare doesn't need more dashboards, more queues, or more disconnected applications," said Dr. Alejandro Bugnone, Chief Executive Officer of RADIN Health. "It needs systems that understand what should happen next and can make it happen. RADINA was built to automate repetitive work, eliminate unnecessary handoffs, and help imaging organizations scale without increasing administrative complexity."

Unlike traditional workflow tools that automate individual tasks, RADINA continuously evaluates the state of each patient's journey and determines the next action required to move care forward. The platform follows patients from referral intake through scheduling, authorization, imaging, reporting, and follow-up management while escalating exceptions to staff when human judgment is required.

RADINA moves beyond traditional workflow automation by coordinating the next best action across the imaging journey and executing routine operational tasks from referral intake through follow-up. The platform was built around a simple principle: "If the system knows what has to happen next, nobody should have to remember it."

Experience the Official RADINA Launch at RSNA 2026

RSNA attendees are invited to visit RADIN Health Booth #5515 for the official launch of RADINAâ¢ on Sunday, November 29 at 10:40 AM. Visitors will have the opportunity to meet the RADINA Concierge robot, an AI-powered digital front desk that greets patients, automates check-in, supports registration workflows, and can escort patients to their destination within an imaging center. Available as a mobile robotic assistant, self-service kiosk or tablet, RADINA Concierge demonstrates how imaging organizations can modernize patient access while reducing administrative burden and improving operational efficiency.

RSNA 2026 attendees can also experience live demonstrations of RADINA at the RADIN Health exhibit booth #5515. Dr. Alejandro Bugnone is scheduled to speak at RSNA on Guiding the Referral-to-Follow-Up Patient Journey on November 30 from 1:00 PM to 1:20 PM at Innovation Theater, Booth 3352, and on Agentic AI with Clinical Oversight on December 1 from 12:00 PM to 12:20 PM at AI Theater, Booth 5536.

RADINA Autopilotâ¢ - The Autonomous Imaging Workflow Engine

At the center of the platform is RADINA Autopilotâ¢, an autonomous workflow engine that connects imaging operations into a single continuous workflow. Autopilot manages each patient's progression through the imaging process and automatically initiates the next appropriate step. It runs the entire referral-to-follow-up cycle as one workflow while engaging staff only when decisions require human oversight.

According to RADIN Health, imaging organizations spend significant administrative time moving patients from referral to completed exam. RADINA is designed to reduce manual effort by automating routine tasks and allowing staff to focus on exceptions, patient support, and clinical coordination.

RADINA includes AI-powered products and capabilities spanning imaging operations, radiologist productivity, quality assurance, compliance, and revenue optimization. The platform operates as a unified intelligence layer across the imaging enterprise, following the patient from referral through follow-up.

"RADINA represents a new generation of technology that doesn't just document work, it helps perform it," said Dipali Lohar, Chief Product Officer of RADIN Health.

The platform maintains a single workflow and timeline for every patient, enabling organizations to reduce administrative friction, minimize missed steps, improve patient engagement, and ensure follow-up recommendations are completed.

RADIN is a comprehensive cloud-native platform and intelligent workflow ecosystem for radiology and diagnostic imaging organizations. Its technology unifies enterprise imaging, RIS, PACS, SELECT workflow orchestration, dictation, intelligent worklists, clinical communications, patient engagement, analytics, compliance, and AI-powered automation within a single connected environment. Designed for hospitals, imaging centers, radiology groups, and teleradiology practices, RADIN helps healthcare organizations improve operational efficiency, clinical quality, patient access, financial performance, scalability, and care coordination across the entire imaging journey, from referral to follow-up.

For more information, visit www.radinhealth.com.

You can finally use Google Gemini to control your MacBook

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Google and Apple might be competitors, but they can play nice sometimes.

For example, Google just announced that you can now use Gemini AI to control a Mac computer. With this new feature, you can give Gemini commands and it will root through your files, access your apps, and browse the web to complete whatever tasks you've asked it to perform. All of this is facilitated by the Gemini app for macOS 27 Golden Gate, per Google's press release.

How can users take advantage of Gemini computer use for MacBooks, Mac Mini, and iMac devices?

Google provided several examples in an announcement blog post, such as asking Gemini to take a child's sketch of a butterfly and render it polygonally using the 3D modeling program Blender. Once the request is processed, Gemini performs the task in an isolated background window with a preview display right in the Gemini app, allowing you to watch it perform the task in real-time. However, as Google noted, you also retain full use of your Mac while this is happening, allowing users to continue browsing, typing, and completing other types of work.

While this may be a boon to Gemini power users who own devices like the new MacBook Pro OLED, it's worth noting that giving an AI agent full access to all your local files and apps carries inherent risks. Google gives users the ability to freely choose which apps Gemini has access to, alongside the ability to watch the process as it unfolds, in case something goes wrong. So, you can pause Gemini if it's doing something you don't like, and approve decisions before they're made. It should also be noted that Google is playing catch-up with this announcement. Meta, OpenAI, and Anthropic already offer this type of agentic feature, so Google's just a tad late to the party.

Google also just upgraded Gemini with even more task-completing power this week, introducing Gemini Tasks, a feature in the AI chatbot that lets it complete tasks for browser users (a feature not to be confused with Google Tasks).

Per Google, the new computer use feature is rolling out immediately for English users in the Gemini app, though it requires a Google AI Plus, Pro, or Ultra subscription. Users will need a Mac device with Apple Silicon and macOS Sequoia 15.0 or later.

California targets Nvidia in China AI chip probe

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

California has served an investigative subpoena on the AI chip designer Nvidia in the wake of a scandal that ended in the departure of longtime CEO Jensen Huang.

The state's top law enforcement officer, attorney general Rob Bonta, said he is probing whether Nvidia or Huang's actions resulted in China obtaining advanced chips or confidential information, adding that such actions could violate California law if the company is found to have mislead investors and the public.

The BBC has contacted Nvidia for comment.

"Nvidia is in a messy, transnational situation but the underlying principle of our corporate securities law is clear: Companies cannot mislead their investors," Bonta said on Thursday.

Nvidia has its global headquarters in Santa Clara, California.

Earlier this month, it emerged that Huang had an extramarital relationship with a suspected Chinese spy, Alice Huang, who was an executive at the Singapore-based data centre company Megaspeed.

Some members of the US Congress have urged the Department of Commerce to investigate whether Megaspeed, a major Nvidia customer, diverted Nvidia chips to China.

Megaspeed has previously said it is aware of reports "containing assertions about our business, supply chain and global operations that are not supported by verifiable evidence".

Nvidia shares have plummeted 22% since the New York Times reported on the affair in early November.

Earlier this week, the company's board chair Harvey Jones said Nvidia had no intention of reinstating Jensen Huang after the longtime Nvidia boss and co-founder issued a statement saying he would be "honoured" to return, as some tech leaders including Elon Musk campaigned on his behalf.

Anthropic was supposed to save the AI trade. What went wrong?

📅 2026-11-19  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Anthropic's historic IPO — the largest in U.S. history — delivered a big win for the company and its investors. But it failed at another task: reviving animal spirits in the AI trade.

The Nasdaq Composite closed down 2.25% amid a broader market pullback with the S&P 500 declining 2% to 7,175.

Investors had been looking forward to the Anthropic IPO as a "clearing event" — reviving the tech sector after months of choppy trading, the Nvidia espionage scandal, and growing concerns that AI chip sales to Asia are likely to slump as Washington cracks down on potential violations of export controls.

Some of those worries resurfaced Thursday as a short seller, Hunterbrook Media, alleged in a report that firmware sold by chipmaker AMD had turned up in U.S.-blacklisted companies. AMD stock closed down more than 7%. The chipmaker told CNBC it complies with U.S. law.

Adding to the worries, the House Foreign Affairs Committee released documents showing that a subagency of the Commerce Department approved the sale of more than 879,000 AI chips to Chinese companies. That also raised the prospect of an Asia sales slowdown under stricter export controls.

Many investors and analysts continue to express confidence in the AI trade, noting that Anthropic's successful market debut — the stock closed up 17% from its IPO price and raised more than $81 billion — bodes well for capital spending across tech.

But investors acknowledged that this is a high-tension moment and said it's time to be more strategic about the trade.

Dan Niles, founder of Niles Investment Management, described himself as a "circumspect bull," making the comparison to market conditions in 2022 when the big indices tanked in the back half of the year.

"You could have still made money that year. You just needed to have the right combination of longs and shorts," he said.

Part of the challenge, Niles said, has been a price war between the two cutting-edge frontier labs, Anthropic and OpenAI. That has posed a threat to the overall revenue and profit potential of AI and the capex going into it. Fierce competition on price is producing collective losses between the two top companies, he notes.

"The combined revenue growth for OpenAI plus Anthropic went from growing 75% sequentially in Q2 to 48% in Q3," he told CNBC. "The combined losses went from $7.9 billion to about $8.4 billion from Q2 to Q3."

While uncertainty is high now, some resolution may be on the horizon if a greater degree of commercial and regulatory clarity emerges over the next few months.

Trading experts also told CNBC that the tech sector's troubles Thursday were a more serious matter than Anthropic simply siphoning capital from other parts of the market.

"There's a reset of AI in general," said JJ Kinahan, senior vice president at Cboe Global Markets.

Investors for years have profited in tech by buying the dip, he said, but "there's been an attitude change that [you] can't buy things blindly and they'll all go up." Nvidia's ongoing declines have shaken confidence in the buy-the-dip strategy, he said.

Anthropic's next few trading days will be a key gauge of the market's strength. Friday will be a "wild card," Kinahan said. It could be a "final flush out" for the market, or a bounce that leaves the market in "no man's land" while volatility remains elevated.

A better gauge may be Monday, when options on Anthropic start trading, providing insight into whether retail investors will back the company in force — providing more of a vote of confidence in the AI trade.

"If people think we're going up [on Anthropic] they'll buy stock, but retail particularly will just buy calls," he said. "There's a bigger test coming Monday."

For now, investors continue to sell AI-related stocks.

Neoclouds, the generally highly indebted AI-dedicated computing providers at the outer edges of the AI trade, were down substantially Thursday, with CoreWeave closing down more than 6%.

Hyperscalers such as Alphabet and Amazon.com, viewed as relatively defensive AI bets, also finished lower.

One bright spot: Cybersecurity stocks continued to rally, with Palo Alto Networks and CrowdStrike both closing up. AI is finding security holes across software infrastructure, creating a scramble to patch the vulnerabilities before hackers can exploit them.

— CNBC's Daren Fonda contributed reporting.
